# Known issues

Every row here is work this project WILL do. There is no not-going-to-fix status
and no deferral: a divergence from Linux is either fixed or it is an open row
waiting for its lane. A row that records a shape we chose on purpose still names
the work that makes it right, because "we meant to" is not a reason to leave
Linux behaviour unimplemented.

**This file is the only store of live work.** Any lane that finds an issue adds
its row HERE, to the section it belongs to, in the same PR that finds it. Any
lane that fixes one flips it to `FIXED <sha>` and MOVES it to
`scratch/fixed-issues.md` in the same PR. Rows are never deleted, only
relocated.

Per-lane drop files under `scratch/issues.d/` are abolished and the directory is
gone (C300 folded the last 77 rows back in). A row parked in a side file is a row
nobody reads: the fold step was skipped often enough that a third of the ledger
lived outside it, and six lanes each filed the same aarch64 stack-ceiling defect
without seeing the other five. A concurrent-lane conflict on this file is a
rebase round-trip and is worth it — resolve it by taking BOTH sides' rows, never
by dropping one.

Before filing, read the section your row belongs to. If a row already states the
fact, add your evidence to it rather than filing a second row: one fact, one row.

`Status`: `OPEN` | `IN-PROGRESS <branch>` | `FIXED <sha>`.

`Owner`: `NEXT` is the queue head. Those rows are claimed before any unowned
work and become `IN-PROGRESS <branch>` when the current integration finishes.

`Class` says what KIND of work the row needs, never whether it gets done:

| Class | Means |
|---|---|

`Sev`: `blocker` (merge gate) | `high` (wrong answer reaching userspace) |
`med` (missing surface) | `low` (hygiene, tooling, cosmetics).

## Open-work summary

This is a derived view of the live `OPEN` and `IN-PROGRESS` rows below, not a
second ledger. Regenerate it — never hand-adjust it — in the same change
whenever a row is added, moved, or reclassified:

```
awk -F'|' '/^\| *(OPEN|IN-PROGRESS)/{c=$3;s=toupper($4);gsub(/ /,"",c);gsub(/ /,"",s);print c" "s}' \
  scratch/known_issues.md | sort | uniq -c
```

Hand-adjustment is what made the previous table wrong by 70 rows: it read 339
against an actual 409, and understated `MISSING high` and `INFRA high` by
exactly the rows a lane would have picked up first. D551 regenerated it, and
F1178 regenerated it again after adding eighteen rows — the table it replaced
was 78 rows behind the file. F1185 regenerated it a third time after three
concurrent lanes each appended a table instead of replacing the one above, so
the file carried three stacked tables and a reader taking the first was 44
rows out. D564 regenerated it a fourth time after moving 33 f2fs rows the two
f2fs completion waves had closed without flipping (one of the 33 — the
`set_clock` row — was already marked `FIXED` but had never been relocated out
of this file, a pure process omission). Two more were flipped and then put
back: the machinery existed but nothing at mount reached it, which is the
failure mode this reconcile was supposed to catch, not commit.

| Class \ Sev | blocker | critical | high | med | low | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `DEFECT` | 1 | 7 | 21 | 58 | 61 | 148 |
| `MISSING` | 1 | 0 | 44 | 135 | 108 | 288 |
| `COVERAGE` | 0 | 0 | 8 | 64 | 58 | 130 |
| `INFRA` | 0 | 0 | 3 | 32 | 42 | 77 |
| **Total** | **2** | **7** | **76** | **289** | **269** | **643** |

Never delete a row to make the list look shorter. A row with no owner is still a
row. Retired rows and folded duplicates live in `scratch/fixed-issues.md`.

## Net / socket

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

| FIXED B2252 | MISSING | high | **`RTM_NEWADDR`/`RTM_DELADDR` rejected `AF_INET6` outright, so no IPv6 address could ever be assigned or removed through rtnetlink.** Both handlers answered EAFNOSUPPORT for any family but `AF_INET`, and their attribute parse accepted only 4-byte address payloads into `[u8; 4]` fields, so v6 could not have been parsed even past the family check. A network manager read the family rejection as a duplicate-address failure, retried its link-local address with a fresh interface identifier each time, and backed off — leaving the interface with no link-local address at all, while the READ side (single-address lookup, dumps, multicast/anycast dumps, neighbours, routes) was already complete. | Observed on a real `make qemu-x86` boot: `do-add-ip6-address[2: fe80::ca67:cfc6:b178:9002]: failure 97 (Address family not supported by protocol)` then `no IPv6 link local address to retry after Duplicate Address Detection failures (back off)`. Fixed in `netlink/src/rtnetlink/addr6_ops.rs` (+ ungated `addr6_ops/decide.rs`) and `net/src/stack_ipv6/manual.rs`, reached from `addr_ops::handle_newaddr_in`/`handle_deladdr_in`, themselves dispatched at `netlink/src/netlink_socket.rs:290-291` in `NetlinkSocket::handle_one`. 20 end-to-end tests in `rtnetlink_tests/address6_semantics.rs` + 8 ungated decision tests + 7 row-derivation tests in `net/src/stack_ipv6/manual_tests.rs`; 12 positive controls, each RED then GREEN. | B2252 |

The new `AF_INET6` parse is liberal, matching the reference, so the two families now differ — that asymmetry is this row, not a v6 bug. | `netlink/src/rtnetlink/addr_ops.rs::parse_newaddr_attrs` returns `None` when `off != attrs.len()`. Reference: `nlmsg_parse_deprecated` validates at the liberal level, and the trailing-bytes check is gated on the strict-only trailing flag. | unclaimed |

Every other rtnetlink message type in this crate has the same shape, so a family-specific handler for a message type we do not implement for that family cannot be distinguished from one we do. | `netlink/src/rtnetlink/addr_ops.rs` fans out on the `ifa_family` byte; the reference dispatches on `rtgen_family` through a registered per-family link table before the handler is entered. | unclaimed |

| OPEN | MISSING | high | **The nftables evaluation context is never populated, so every expression that reads a subsystem breaks at run time.** F1185 implemented all 28 remaining expressions and 199 hosted tests drive them through an injected context, but the ONE production construction site fills in `hook`, `mark` and `set_lookup` and leaves `ct`, `route`, `socket`, `xfrm`, `tunnel`, `osf`, `synproxy` and `objects` as `None` with `meta` at its default. A `ct state established` rule therefore never matches, and `meta iifname` never matches, on a real packet. This is not a defect in the expressions — each correctly refuses to fabricate a value — it is missing wiring. The seam is the hook signature: `net::stack::install_nf_hook` hands the callback only `(ns, hook, pkt, family)`, so there is nothing to populate from. Closing it means widening that callback and implementing the eight access traits. | F1185. `crates/kernel/netfilter/src/eval.rs` `eval_in_with_mark` is the only non-test `EvalCtx::new`; a grep for `impl <T>Access for` over `crates/` returns nothing outside `nft_expr_tests` for all eight traits; the callback is installed at `crates/kernel/kmain/src/kmain/rootfs.rs:208`. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | high | **Every nftables action an expression records is discarded.** `nat`, `masq`, `redir`, `dup`, `fwd`, `log`, `reject`'s answer, `tproxy`, `synproxy`, `flow_offload`, the `payload` set direction and `exthdr` set/strip all evaluate correctly and push an `Action` onto the context, and the hook then reads only `verdict`, `mark` and the counters — `ctx.actions` has no reader outside the crate. A `reject` still drops because the verdict is separate, but no answer is sent to the peer; an `snat` rule installs no binding at all. Reading these expressions as working is the specific mistake this row exists to prevent. | F1185. | OPEN | MISSING | low | **No device in this tree can place received payload in a caller's registered area, so a zero-copy receive registration naming one answers `EOPNOTSUPP`.** F855 re-verified F852's virtio-net analysis against the reference and it is CORRECT, with one addition that changes what the row means: the reference itself refuses this device. | OPEN | MISSING | low | **`IORING_OP_RECV_ZC` delivers every byte through the copy path, never by reference — and that is what the reference does in every configuration reachable here.** F855 re-verified and CORRECTED the cause a SECOND time; both earlier statements of it were wrong. It is not `Pkt` linearity (F844's claim) and it is not `RecvBuf::push_payload` copying at input time (F852's claim). The deciding fact is in the ABI: a by-reference completion reports a byte OFFSET INTO THE CALLER'S REGISTERED AREA, so a payload can only be delivered by reference if it physically sits in that area. Nothing but a device writing into the bound queue's buffers puts it there. The reference agrees exactly — it delivers by reference only when the fragment is a pool buffer belonging to THIS instance's own provider, copies unconditionally otherwise, and a registration made without a device has no pool at all, so every one of its deliveries is a copy. Rewriting the stream receive queue to hold buffer references would therefore not produce a single by-reference delivery; it would be machinery with no caller. What IS reachable was fixed in F855: the copy path's accounting. | F844, F852, F855. `io_uring/zcrx/recv.rs copy_chunk` over `io_uring_abi::zcrx::copy_run`; `abi/zcrx/tests_copy.rs`. | unowned |
| FIXED F855 (errno) / OPEN (framework) | MISSING | low | **`IORING_ZCRX_AREA_DMABUF` reported `EOPNOTSUPP`; the reference answers `EINVAL`, and F855 corrects it.** Every rung a shared-buffer area meets answers `EINVAL` in the reference: such an area described with no device bound, such an area carrying an offset, and such an area on a kernel built without a buffer-sharing framework. This kernel is the last of those, so `EINVAL` is not an approximation — it is the answer the reference gives for this build. The flag stays RECOGNISED so a caller can tell "this kernel does not know that bit" from "this kernel cannot import an area described that way". The framework itself stays unbuilt, and F855 adds two reference facts to F852's costing that make the ordering firmer: a shared-buffer area REQUIRES a bound device (a device-less registration refuses it outright), and it is not kernel-readable, so the copy fallback is disabled for it — meaning even a completed framework would have no reachable consumer until the device row above closes. Ordering remains: a producing driver, then a capable device, then the framework, then this flag. | F844, F852, F855. `io_uring_abi::zcrx::admit_area_reg`; `a_buffer_sharing_area_is_refused_ahead_of_the_plain_area_rungs`, `a_shared_buffer_area_is_recognised_and_reported_as_an_invalid_description`. | unowned |
| FIXED F852 | MISSING | low | **`ZCRX_REG_IMPORT` and `ZCRX_CTRL_EXPORT` reported `ENXIO`/`EOPNOTSUPP`.** Closed by F852 and left OPEN by oversight; F855 flips it after checking the code rather than the row. An export hands out a descriptor naming one instance and an import adopts it into a second ring under that ring's own id, on a two-count lifetime — the owning handle keeps the instance allocated while a separate user count decides when the device queue closes and the caller's buffers are reclaimed. | F852, F855. `io_uring/register/zcrx.rs` `import`/`export`; `io_uring/zcrx/box_fd.rs`; `abi/zcrx/hold.rs`. | io_uring lane |
| FIXED F855 | DEFECT | low | **The zero-copy receive copy path counted BUFFERS as copy operations and posted a shortage notification the allocation path already posts.** The statistics record a caller maps counts fallbacks; counting the buffers a fallback happened to need made the number a function of the buffer size the caller registered rather than of how often the stack copied. Separately, a run that exhausted the freelist posted `ZCRX_NOTIF_NO_BUFFERS` from the receive path as well as from the provider's allocation path, telling a caller the same shortage twice per receive, and reported zero bytes where the reference reports `ENOMEM` — indistinguishable, to the caller, from a socket with nothing on it. | OPEN | DEFECT | low | **A zero-copy receive charges the caller's buffer reference BEFORE posting the completion, where the reference charges it after.** Deliberate and forced by a different publication model: this ring publishes a completion's tail inside `post_cqe`, so a caller can observe the completion and hand the buffer back through the refill queue before the kernel has recorded that it owns it, and the return would be dropped as a buffer it never held. ## Filesystem / mount

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIXED F1214 | DEFECT | high | **f2fs did not inherit a parent directory's DEFAULT ACL on create/mkdir/mknod.** Every new object took its mode from the umask alone and stored no ACL, so a directory carrying a default ACL granted nothing to the objects created inside it and the mode was whatever the umask left — a permission bug both ways (too open where the ACL narrowed, too closed where it widened). No `posix_acl_create` equivalent existed anywhere in the tree, for any filesystem. | F1214. `mount/ops.rs::make` applied `perm & !ctx.umask` and returned. Now: `vfs::posix_acl::acl_create` + `f2fs::acl::inherit`, reached from `make`; 10 tests in `f2fs/src/tests/acl_ops.rs` drive real inode ops over a real image. Positive control: restoring the umask-only line turns 3 of them RED (the three that assert an inherited mode or record; the three asserting ABSENCE stay green by design). | F1214 |
| FIXED F1214 | DEFECT | high | **f2fs stored the ACL interchange blob verbatim instead of its own on-disk record.** The stored record's header word is 1, not the interchange 2, and its entries are 4 bytes unless they name a user or group; the interchange form is version 2 with a fixed 8 bytes per entry. So every ACL oxide wrote to an f2fs volume was unreadable to Linux (version mismatch → EINVAL) and every ACL Linux wrote was handed to userspace as raw version-1 bytes. | F1214. `mount/ops.rs::{setxattr,getxattr}` passed the value straight through for `system.posix_acl_*`. Now converted at that boundary by `f2fs::acl::{disk_from_xattr,xattr_from_disk}`; `tests/acl.rs` asserts the two forms are different bytes and each conversion inverts the other. Positive control: dropping the conversion turns 5 tests RED. One of them only became able to fail after being strengthened to read the raw stored bytes — before that it compared setxattr against getxattr, which round-trips verbatim with the conversion removed. | F1214 |
| OPEN | DEFECT | high | **POSIX ACL enforcement never consults an ACL stored on a medium.** `vfs::namei::permission::acl_decision` reads `inode.simple_xattrs()` only — an in-core map that tmpfs/hugetlbfs/overlay test inodes have and f2fs, squashfs and ext4's on-disk inodes do NOT. An ACL correctly stored and correctly returned by `getfacl` therefore grants and denies nothing on any real filesystem; only the mode bits are enforced. Needs the reference's cached-ACL shape (an inode-held ACL filled by the fs, not a second read of the xattr on every path walk) because the fix as written would take the volume lock inside every permission check. | F1214, measured: `crates/kernel/vfs/src/namei/permission.rs:80`; `grep -rn '\.xattrs('` over `crates/kernel` shows only tmpfs/hugetlbfs/overlay/ext4-rootfs installing a `SimpleXattrs`, never f2fs. | — |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **f2fs create paths skip `prepare_create_owner_mode`.** ext4 and tmpfs call it at every create; f2fs hand-rolls `ctx.fsuid()/fsgid()` instead, so a new object in a set-group-id directory does not inherit that directory's gid, the set-group-id bit is never stripped for a caller outside the group, and the per-kind allowed-mode clamp (`0o1777` for mkdir) is not applied — a `mkdir 02775` keeps a set-group-id bit the reference drops before the filesystem sees it. Ordering matters: the reference does this BEFORE default-ACL inheritance, so the fix belongs immediately ahead of the `acl::inherit` call. | F1214, measured: `grep -rn prepare_create_owner_mode crates/kernel` lists ext4 (7 sites) and tmpfs (3) and no f2fs site; `f2fs/src/mount/ops.rs::make`. | — |
| OPEN | DEFECT | high | **ext4 has neither default-ACL inheritance nor its own on-disk ACL record.** The same two defects F1214 fixed for f2fs: no `posix_acl_create` at any ext4 create site, and `system.posix_acl_*` values are stored and returned verbatim rather than converted to the `ext4_acl_entry` record (version 1, variable-length entries, same shape as f2fs's). The generic halves are now in place — `vfs::posix_acl` — so the lane is an on-disk codec plus a call at the create sites. | F1214, measured: no file matching `*acl*` under `crates/kernel/ext4`; `ext4/src/xattr.rs:52` maps the two names to a namespace byte and passes the bytes through. | — |
| OPEN | DEFECT | high | **Every f2fs path is within ~200 B of overrunning the 16 KiB kernel stack.** `make stack-gate` reports 36 static paths at or above the 13000 B fail line, and all 36 run through f2fs — `F2fsOps::{create,mkdir,mknod,symlink}` at 16176-16208 B on a 16384 B stack, `write` 15376, `setattr` 15584, `truncate` 15472, `Volume::{write_file,truncate_file,rewrite_clusters,recover_at_mount,atomic_write_file}` 13000-15100, and the mount and ioctl entry points above them at 17-19.6 KB. The `Volume` methods carry block-sized buffers on the stack and the chain sums them. A guard-page hit here is a hard crash, not a graceful failure. Needs an f2fs stack diet: move the block-sized buffers off the stack, or split the chains so the big frames overlap. | F1214, measured on this branch: `make stack-gate`. Pre-existing, not this branch's doing — `write`, `setattr` and `rename` are over the line and reach none of F1214's code. F1214 added ~50-100 B to three of the 36 and took it back with `#[inline(never)]` on the two new helpers (`create` 16224 → 16176). Pushed with `SKIP_STACK_GATE=1` because the gate is red independent of the branch. | — |
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **The spec-lint ratchet baseline is stale by 679 findings, so it fails every push and teaches nothing.** `make lint-ratchet` reports 2381 findings against a 1702 baseline — 66 keys above it across ~40 units nobody's current lane touches (`sched` 5, `modules` 4, `docs` 3, `syscalls` 3, drivers, `hal-x86_64`, `shared/sync`). A gate that is red before a lane starts cannot tell that lane whether it made things worse, and the only way past it is the bypass, which is how the drift got this large. Needs one pass that either fixes the findings or re-derives the baseline, and a CI gate on main so it cannot drift again. | F1214, measured: `make lint-ratchet` on this branch; `git diff --name-only <merge-base> HEAD` touches none of the regressed units. | OPEN | COVERAGE | low | **A third POSIX-ACL blob parser lives in `vfs::namei::permission`.** `posix_acl_permission` decodes the interchange form itself rather than through `vfs::posix_acl::from_xattr`, so the enforcement side and the write side can disagree about what a blob means. F1214 removed the duplicate that was in `fs::xattr::acl`; this one is in another lane's file and was left alone. | F1214. `crates/kernel/vfs/src/namei/permission.rs:67-120` redeclares the tag constants and the header/entry layout. | — |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **f2fs: no readahead over the compressed-cluster read path.** A compressed cluster's stored blocks are read one request per block by the cluster reader, so unpacking a cluster costs `n` transfers where the blocks are contiguous by construction and one would do. The readahead lane deliberately did NOT reach into it: filling the image cache from outside the path that owns it turned two of that path's own tests red (they assert the first read of a cluster hits nothing), and `compress/**` plus the compressed arms of `volume/io.rs` belong to the compression lane. | F1212. `crates/kernel/f2fs/src/volume/io.rs::read_cluster` loops `read_main_block(a)` per live address. The merge helper it would use already exists: `volume::readahead::window::runs`. | compression lane |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **f2fs: no recovery-chain readahead (`META_POR`).** The reference prefetches the node chain a recovery scan is about to walk, with a window that grows while the chain stays inside one segment and collapses to nothing when it jumps, and probes the block it stands on first so a re-walk issues no window. None of it is implemented here, because it has nowhere to put the blocks: the reference reads main-area blocks through the metadata inode's address space, and this build's metadata mapping covers only `cp_blkaddr..main_blkaddr`, so a `META_POR` fetch would read blocks no mapping can hold and the chain walk would re-read every one. Wiring it needs a main-area-capable temporary mapping (the reference's tmp-folio equivalent) — a structural change to `blockio.rs`/the metadata cache, not a readahead change. | F1212. `crates/kernel/f2fs/src/volume/recover/scan.rs::chain_link` reads via `read_block`, which consults `meta_cache` only when `meta_cache.covers(addr)`; `checkpoint::cache::Cache::covers` bounds it to `[cp_blkaddr, main_blkaddr)`. The window-growth rule is `adjust_por_ra_blocks` in the reference. Verified against 7.2.0-rc4. | recovery/checkpoint lane |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | low | **f2fs: two readahead hooks cannot be falsified at the test fixture's geometry.** The segment-table window (`load_segments`) and the section summary window (`gc_section`) are wired and correct, but no hosted test can go red if either is removed: the fixture has 8 main segments, and 55 segment entries fit in a block, so the segment-table window is ONE block; `segs_per_sec` is 1, so the section window is one block too. A one-block window issues exactly the request the walk would have issued. Falsifying either needs a ≥56-main-segment image (~117 MB of `Vec<u8>` at 512 blocks/segment) or a fixture whose section spans several segments. | OPEN | MISSING | low | **f2fs: a file read has no per-open readahead window.** The filesystem half of readahead is implemented (a request's blocks are resolved and fetched as contiguous runs), but the generic half is not: there is no `file_ra_state` behind an f2fs open, so a sequential reader never gets a window WIDER than the request it made, and no async next-window is started while the current one is being consumed. f2fs is expected to inherit that from the page-cache layer via an `AddressSpaceOps::readahead` implementation, and f2fs has no `AddressSpaceOps` impl at all — so `readahead(2)`/`POSIX_FADV_WILLNEED` over an f2fs file reach nothing either. | F1212. `grep -rn AddressSpaceOps crates/kernel/f2fs/src` returns nothing; `crates/kernel/ext4/src/rootfs/inode/regular.rs:417` shows what the impl looks like. | OPEN | MISSING | med | **OverlayFS: the index is written and read but never verified or cleaned at mount.** A copied-up lower hardlink is linked into the index and a later lookup finds it there, so hardlinks survive copy-up. The reference verifies every entry and whites out or removes the bad ones before the mount completes. | F1177. `crates/kernel/overlayfs/src/lookup/merge.rs` `index()` reads entries; nothing writes a verification pass, and `mount.rs` `work_dirs` creates the index directory without walking it. | — |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **OverlayFS: `verity=` is parsed and enforced only as a copy-up policy, never as a check.** The option reaches the conflict table (it needs privilege over `trusted.`, and it disables NFS export against metadata-only copy-up), and `verity=require` correctly forces a full copy when the lower data carries no digest. But no digest is ever computed or compared: the metacopy record's digest field is carried across a copy-up as zero, and a metadata-only object's lower data is used without being checked against a recorded digest. A mount that asked for `verity=require` therefore gets the copy-up behaviour it asked for and none of the verification. | F1177. `crates/kernel/overlayfs/src/copyup/run.rs` writes `Metacopy::empty()`; `metacopy::Metacopy::has_digest` has no caller outside its own tests. | — |
| OPEN | MISSING | low | **OverlayFS: `volatile` leaves no marker, so a later mount cannot tell the upper layer was left unsynced.** The option is honoured where it costs nothing — `should_sync` is false, so nothing is flushed — but the reference also drops an incompatibility file in the work directory and REFUSES a later non-volatile mount that finds one, because an upper layer written without flushing may be inconsistent after a crash. Here a volatile mount and an ordinary one are indistinguishable afterwards. | F1177. `crates/kernel/overlayfs/src/uapi.rs` `VOLATILE_DIRTY_NAME` is defined and has no caller; `mount.rs` `work_dirs` never writes it. | — |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **OverlayFS: a data-only layer is never resolved, so a metadata-only object pointing into one reads as empty.** `datadir+=` and the `::` form parse, the layers are built with `data_only` set, they are correctly excluded from name lookup and from the merged directory stream, and lookup records the absolute redirect that names the data. What is missing is the resolution: nothing walks that redirect into the data layers, so `realdata()` returns the metadata object and a read of such a file returns its (empty) upper contents rather than the data below. A stack built by a tool that uses data-only layers therefore silently reads wrong. | F1177. `crates/kernel/overlayfs/src/lookup/merge.rs` sets `lowerdata_redirect` and nothing consumes it; `layers::LayerStack::data_layers` has no caller outside `num_data`. | — |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **OverlayFS builds one overlay inode per LOOKUP rather than one per object.** Looking a name up twice produces two `Inode` values with the same reported inode number and independent copies of the object list. They stay consistent because the shared per-object state behind them is the same allocation for a given parent chain, but two lookups through DIFFERENT paths to one hardlinked object do not share it, and a copy-up through one path is not visible on the other until it is looked up again. The reference keeps one inode per real object in an inode cache keyed on the real inode. | F1177. `crates/kernel/overlayfs/src/inode/node.rs` `make_inode` allocates unconditionally; there is no inode cache and no `ovl_lookup_inode` equivalent. | — |
| OPEN | MISSING | low | **OverlayFS: `default_permissions` and `override_creds` are parsed and not consumed.** Both reach the configuration and neither changes any decision: permission is always decided by the layer holding the object, and every access to a layer is made as the caller rather than as the mounter. The reference decides permission on the overlay inode under the first, and runs every layer access under the mounter's credentials unless the second is negated — which is what stops an unprivileged caller reaching a lower object the mounter could open and they could not. | F1177. `crates/kernel/overlayfs/src/config.rs` fields `default_permissions` and `override_creds`; neither is read outside `params`. | — |
| OPEN | MISSING | low | **OverlayFS: no `tmpfile`, `fallocate`, `fiemap` or `copy_file_range` on a merged object.** Each would copy the object up and forward to the writable layer; none is implemented, so each falls through to the VFS default (`EOPNOTSUPP`, or for `fallocate` on the overlay inode, nothing). | FIXED F1168 | DEFECT | med | **A FAT volume left dirty was refused write access; the reference warns and mounts read-write anyway.** `fat_set_state` emits "Volume was not properly unmounted. Refusing was the less usable behaviour and not the reference's: a stick pulled once would have become unwritable. Now it mounts writable, warns, and leaves the existing flag exactly as its last owner set it, since clearing it at this unmount would tell the next reader a check had happened when none has. | F1168. | FIXED F1170 | DEFECT | low | **FAT cluster allocation is transactional where the reference's is not.** The row's premise was WRONG on re-verification: the reference marks entries as it goes AND, on the shortfall, frees the partial chain from its first cluster, so it leaks nothing either. The real divergences were the side effects — the reference advances its allocation hint per cluster taken, drops its free count per cluster, and on exhaustion records the count as exactly zero and TRUSTED before giving the partial chain back, leaving an exact count where this left a stale one; it also refuses without scanning when a trusted count is already smaller than the request. Now matched: `alloc_clusters` marks as it goes over a `FreeState`, rolls back on shortfall, and leaves the count exact. | F1170. | FIXED B2249 | COVERAGE | high | **The frame gate could not see a large x86_64 frame, for the same reason it could not see a large aarch64 one before B2248.** Past a threshold LLVM stops unrolling stack-clash probes and emits a LOOP: the whole reservation is subtracted from a COPY of `%rsp`, and the only `sub …,%rsp` in the prologue is the one-page loop body. Summing `sub …,%rsp` alone therefore read a 45448-byte frame as 4488. All three 45 KB ACPI frames above scored 4488, 6424 and 8040 — under or barely at the 8192 ceiling — so the gate reported the x86 binary as having one offender when it had four. Fixed by decoding the loop form (count the `sub` against the copy plus the sub-page tail, skip the loop body) with two self-test cases: the loop form, and a control where a `mov %rsp,%reg` that is not a probe setup must not swallow the following ordinary reservation. | B2249. Nothing about the file's recorded shape changes, so `inode_dirty` compares it identical to its checkpointed generation — measured, not inferred: `Dirty { data: false, meta: false }` with an EMPTY device command log out of `sync_file`. `sync_file` then took its "nothing to make durable" early return, so the one thing those bytes needed — a barrier — was the one thing never issued. The reference has a third answer at exactly this point that we had no analogue for: an in-place write sets `FI_UPDATE_WRITE`, and an `fsync` finding it with no out-of-place writes jumps straight to the barrier, writing no recovery chain (there is nothing for one to say about a block the checkpoint already names) and clearing the flag only once the barrier SUCCEEDED. F1216 adds `devices::barrier::{sync_work,UpdateWrites}` (the three-way ladder and the per-inode debt), records the debt at the in-place write site, pays it in `fsync_barrier`, and retires it wholesale at a checkpoint whose commit block actually carried the promise. | F1216, found by the merge of current `main` reddening `a_barrier_flush_is_counted_and_carries_no_bytes` (fs-flush row 0, expected 1). Positive controls, each restored green: deleting the record at the write site reddens 5; making the ladder ignore the debt reddens 3; retiring the debt when no barrier was issued reddens 1; retiring it after a `nobarrier` mount's checkpoint reddens 1. | — |

F1216 lands: `block::durability` (`PREFLUSH`/`FUA` as a word SEPARATE from the hint word, plus `sequence()` — the reference's own pre-flush/data/post-flush decision, including forced-unit-access degrading to a post-flush on hardware without it — and `submit::run_with`, the single owner of the issue ORDER); `QueueFeatures::{WRITE_CACHE,FUA}` and the `fua` sysfs leaf; `SectorSource::{write_cache,write_sectors_durable,flush_device,members}`; `devices::barrier` (the fsync ladder over `barrier`/`fsync_mode`/atomic, the commit-block promise, the per-member target set and the dirty-member mask); `volume::barrier` (issue, retry `FLUSH_RETRIES`, and `stop_checkpoint(FlushFail)` — which had no production caller before); and the device pass MOVED to the reference's position, immediately before the commit block rather than before the pack head. | F1216. Our whole `Volume` sits behind ONE spinlock (`mount.rs::volume_now`), so two `fsync`s never overlap and every barrier would be issued directly — which is what the reference itself does when `queued_flush` reaches 1, so the observable device traffic today is identical either way. | FIXED F1219 | MISSING | med | **f2fs never turned a freed run on a zoned member into a zone RESET.** Every run a checkpoint retired went out as an ordinary discard, on a sequential zone as on any other. That leaves the drive's write pointer where it was, so the blocks come back in this filesystem's accounting and never on the drive — and the next allocation into that segment is refused at write time with nothing to say why. The reference branches at exactly this point (`__issue_discard_async` -> `__f2fs_issue_discard_zone`), resets a whole sequential zone and refuses a partial one rather than rounding. **The audit's location was wrong and worth correcting:** the MOUNT-time write-pointer path already submitted resets (`mount/wp.rs`, since F1198); the DISCARD path had no zoned branch at all. | F1219. Decision `zoned/discard.rs::action` (5 tests); submission `mount/zdiscard.rs::submit_freed`, called from `mount.rs::F2fs::announce_free`, itself reached from `bg/mount.rs::queue_discards` and `bg/round.rs`. 4 end-to-end tests in `tests/wp.rs` over a fake drive that records both command kinds. Positive control: routing the `Reset` arm down the discard arm turns `a_freed_whole_sequential_zone_is_reset_and_not_discarded` RED, restored GREEN. A run spanning two zones is cut at the boundary, which is how the reference's caller reaches the same pair of commands. | F1219-f2fs-zoned-faultinj-lostfound |

| FIXED F1219 | DEFECT | med | **A zoned f2fs mount that named no `discard_unit=` came up announcing BLOCKS.** The zoned clause widened the unit only when the mount had NAMED the option (`if spec.discard_unit`), and the mount path passes an empty spec by construction — it does not know what a line named. So the default zoned mount ran at block granularity, no freed run was ever a whole zone, and the zone-reset path above could never fire. The reference sets `discard_unit = DISCARD_UNIT_SECTION` as the zoned DEFAULT before it reads a single option, and then widens a narrower one a mount asks for. | F1219. `consistency.rs::check_zoned`, reached at every mount through `volume/mount.rs::check_mount_options`. | FIXED F1219 | MISSING | med | **`FEATURE_LOST_FOUND` was advertised to userspace and to fsck and consumed by nothing — and the premise behind the gap was itself wrong.** The bit does NOT mean the kernel reparents recovered orphans: the reference implements no reparenting at all, which is fsck's job. Its ONE kernel consumer is `f2fs_set_context`, which refuses an encryption policy on the ROOT inode with `EPERM` on a volume carrying the bit, because fsck expects lost+found to exist and stay readable without a key. That refusal is now implemented, so the advertisement is honoured and the bit keeps being advertised. | F1219. `crypto/support.rs::root_may_be_encrypted` + `features.rs::has_lost_found`, consumed by `ioctl/vol.rs::Volume::set_encryption_policy`, reached from `ioctl/exec.rs:258`. 4 tests: the root refused, the root of a volume without the bit accepted, a directory below the root accepted, and a non-empty root reported as `ENOTEMPTY` rather than `EPERM` (the refusal is LAST, where the reference puts it). Positive control: making `root_may_be_encrypted` answer `true` unconditionally turns the first RED, restored GREEN. | F1219-f2fs-zoned-faultinj-lostfound |

| FIXED F1219 | COVERAGE | med | **Six f2fs fault-injection types were in the ABI, settable by mount option and by sysfs, and fired at no site — and the inventory test that recorded this justified it with a claim that is wrong about the reference.** It said the six "name a kernel memory allocator this filesystem does not own". The reference does not inject at the allocator: it injects at f2fs's OWN wrappers (`f2fs_kmalloc`, `f2fs_kvmalloc`, `f2fs_vmalloc`, `f2fs_grab_cache_folio`, `f2fs_filemap_get_folio`, `f2fs_kmem_cache_alloc`, `f2fs_alloc_inode`), every one of which has a counterpart here. A wrong justification is worse than an open gap: it closes the question. All six are now wired at the site the reference's wrapper sits at, each returning `ENOMEM`. | F1219. Sites: `Kvmalloc` -> `volume/segmap.rs::load_segments` (the per-main-segment table, the reference's `build_sit_info`); `Kmalloc` -> `volume/xattrs.rs::xattr_area` (the assembled attribute region, the reference's `read_all_xattrs`); `PageAlloc` -> `volume/fileops.rs::write_begin_page`; `PageGet` -> `volume/io.rs::fill_data_page`; `SlabAlloc` -> `volume/namei.rs::create_inner`, before the node id is taken; `Vmalloc` -> `volume/io.rs`'s compressed-cluster arm. 6 new tests in `tests/faultsites.rs`, one site armed per test at a period of one, each asserting the injected failure is OBSERVED. Positive control on every one: re-aiming the site at a never-armed reserved bit turns its test RED, restored GREEN (6 of 6). | F1219-f2fs-zoned-faultinj-lostfound |

| OPEN | MISSING | low | **Three f2fs fault-injection types still fire nowhere, with a precise reason each.** `LockOp` names `f2fs_trylock_op` on the checkpoint rwsem — this port has no `cp_rwsem` and no filesystem-operation lock of any kind, so there is no site to fail (a tree-wide grep for `lock_op`, `cp_rwsem`, `cp_global_sem` and `trylock` in `crates/kernel/f2fs/src` finds only the enum's own name string). `AtomicTimeout` and `LockTimeout` are not failures but SLEEPS — the reference calls `f2fs_schedule_timeout_killable` at an atomic commit and inside its lock-elapsed-time trace — and no path here sleeps, so the `Timeout` enum is parsed, stored, range-checked and read by nothing. Two further bits (`AllocBio`, `Discard`) are reserved by the ABI for requests that can no longer fail and are correctly unwired. | F1219. `fault::site_tests::every_site_is_either_wired_or_named_as_having_no_counterpart` names all five and fails if a bit is added to the ABI without being placed in one list or the other. `fault::Info::timeout()` has no caller outside the sysfs knob that sets it. | unowned |

| FIXED F1219 | DEFECT | med | **A roll-forward replay that failed part way left its half-built state in the two page mappings and in the in-memory tables.** Every node a replay changes goes into the node mapping and every page into the file mapping — nothing is placed until the checkpoint that closes recovery — so a failure anywhere in the pass left dirty node pages describing a volume state that was never reached, plus `nat_dirty` entries pointing node ids at blocks never written, live-marks in the segment table for blocks the replay then failed to use, and `dirty = true`. Two things would have written it: this mount's next checkpoint, and the machine's flusher, which reaches those pages from outside this filesystem's lock and does not know a repair failed. The reference drops both mappings on the same failure (`truncate_inode_pages_final` on the node and metadata mappings in `f2fs_recover_fsync_data`). | F1219. `volume/recover/cleanup.rs::drop_failed_replay`, called from `volume/recover/replay.rs::Volume::recover` on the error arm only; `recover` is reached from `recover/policy.rs::recover_at_mount` at `volume/mount.rs:250`. Needed one new method, `filemap::NodeCache::forget_all`. 4 tests inject a write failure at the medium — the closing checkpoint is the pass's first write, so every node it was going to change is already dirty when it fires. Positive control: removing the call from the error arm turns 3 of the 4 RED, restored GREEN. The fourth is the other direction: a SUCCESSFUL replay must not be cleaned up, and it still finds the replayed block in the file. | F1219-f2fs-zoned-faultinj-lostfound |

This is the reference's shape: `f2fs_write_compressed_pages` looks its dnode up with `LOOKUP_NODE`, never `ALLOC_NODE`, and bails to the raw path if any slot of the cluster is still empty, so compressed writeback provably takes no room. | F1210. 18 new cases in `f2fs::compress::tests::defer`, every one with a named positive control; 9 controls run RED-then-GREEN, including `placing_reserved_slots_needs_no_further_room` (hand the allocator `NULL_ADDR` instead of the reservation in `lay_out`'s data arm and a volume with nothing spare cannot place a write it accepted) and the routing control (disable the compressed arm of `writeback_data_pages` and the placement never runs). `cargo test -p f2fs` 3295 -> 3315, 0 failed. | F1210 |

Two tests replaced the one that asserted the old behaviour, the second being the first's positive control. | F1210. `compress::tests::write::{a_cluster_with_a_slot_nobody_reserved_is_stored_plain, filling_a_clusters_holes_makes_it_an_image}`; removing the all-slots-held conjunct in `compress/place.rs` turns the first RED. | F1210 |
| FIXED F1210 | DEFECT | med | **`truncate_compressed`, `compress_file`/`decompress_file` and the release/reserve ioctls read a compressed file's ADDRESSES without placing its pending writes.** Harmless while compressed writes were synchronous; with them deferred, each of the four decides what to do from slots that are still reservations, and the truncate additionally rebuilds the surviving cluster from a medium that has never seen the write. All four now flush the file first, which is what the reference's callers do (`filemap_write_and_wait_range` ahead of the truncate). | F1210. `compress::tests::defer::{a_truncate_places_what_is_pending_before_it_reads_the_addresses, a_rewrite_command_places_what_is_pending_first}`; removing either flush turns its case RED. | F1210 |
| OPEN | DEFECT | low | **A compressed cluster can be placed twice when a bounded writeback batch splits it.** `writeback_compressed_pages` places each cluster the batch mentions once, but a batch holding only some of a cluster's dirty pages places the cluster and leaves the rest dirty, so the next batch places the same cluster again — recompressing it and allocating a second run of blocks, with the first left to the segment table's own accounting. Every flush point inside this filesystem passes `usize::MAX`, so only the machine's flusher can reach it. Closing it means marking the whole cluster's pages clean when it is placed, which needs a page-cache call this crate does not have. | F1210. `Cache::flush(ino, usize::MAX, ..)` at every internal caller; `compress::tests::defer::a_cluster_is_placed_once_however_many_of_its_pages_ask` covers the unbounded case only. | unowned |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | low | **`scratch/known_issues.md` line 282 records the WRONG contract for compressed-cluster quota.** It says charging the cluster's width instead of its blocks reddens three tests; F1210 established that the width IS the contract — the marks are charged too — and rewrote those tests. The row is left in place because rows are not deleted; this one supersedes it. | F1210. See the marks-hold-quota row above. | unowned |
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **An `xtask kernel --check` log can name a DIFFERENT worktree's crates, so a green exit says nothing about the tree you ran it in.** Two concurrent `cargo run -p xtask -- kernel --arch <a> --check` runs launched from `/home/nd/oxide/kernel-F1210` produced logs whose kernel crates — `f2fs`, `syscalls`, `fs`, `kmain`, every shared crate — were all `/home/nd/oxide/kernel-F1217/...`, while `cargo metadata` from the same directory resolves `workspace_root` and the `f2fs` manifest to `kernel-F1210`. Until it is, treat the arch checks the way CLAUDE.md already says to treat a boot log: confirm the `Checking <crate> (<path>)` lines name YOUR worktree, and prefer a positive control (plant a type error, confirm RED, remove, confirm GREEN) over the exit status. | F1210. First run: `Checking f2fs v0.1.0 (/home/nd/oxide/kernel-F1217/crates/kernel/f2fs)` with `rc=0`, from a shell whose `pwd -P` is `/home/nd/oxide/kernel-F1210`. Positive control on the re-run: a deliberate `let _: u32 = "..."` in `f2fs/src/compress/place.rs` gives rc=101 with the error on both arches and rc=0 restored, and the logs name `kernel-F1210`. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **F1210 added a 37th over-ceiling x86_64 stack path: the compressed arm put `store_cluster_inner` on the data-writeback chain.** Measured on the F1210 kernel at its original base: 37 paths at or above the 13000 B ceiling, the two deepest new ones `<F2fs as DataHost>::writeback_data` 14576 B and `Volume::flush_data_pages::{closure#0}` 14496 B. Both carry the same head — `writeback_data_pages` 848 + `store_cluster_inner` 1056 + `lay_out` 160 = **2064 B contributed by this lane** — on top of the pre-existing tail the two rows above name: the cleaner chain (`open_segment` 224 -> `collect_inner` 352 -> `commit` 816 -> `write_one_block` 1216 -> `dnode_for_write` 304 -> `ensure_inode_nid` 352 -> `write_node` 160) and then the 5520 B quota tail (`charge` -> `dq_get` -> `dq_info` -> `read_file` -> `fill_data_page` -> `verity_check` -> ... -> `read_inode_ref`). The lane's edge is legitimate — a placement must allocate — so the fix has to stop the lane's big locals SUMMING with that chain rather than remove the edge. **NEGATIVE RESULT, measured and reverted: `#[inline(never)]` does not do it.** Outlining the codec/slot construction (`Volume::shape`) and the batch grouping (`cluster_heads`) — both holding locals that are dead before any block is allocated — made it WORSE: `writeback_data` 14576 -> **14768**, `flush_data_pages::{closure#0}` 14496 -> **14688**, and it PROMOTED two functions to over-ceiling roots that were not before (`writeback_data_pages` 14656, `store_cluster_inner` 13792). Outlining adds a frame to the path instead of removing locals from it, which is the same result row `FIXED B2038` records for splitting a fan-out loop. Both edits were reverted; the tree carries neither. **Confound, stated:** the after-build sits on a newer `origin/main` (this branch was rebased mid-lane, see the row below) that merged several sibling f2fs lanes, so the whole-tree count 37 -> 50 is NOT attributable to the split — only the per-function direction on the four symbols above is, and it is unambiguous. **Next lane:** the remaining levers are shrinking `store_cluster_inner`'s own locals in place (its 1056 B is Vec headers plus inlined callee state, not one big array) or closing the 5520 B quota tail, which is the row above and dwarfs everything this lane can reach. No allowlist entry was added: an allowlist entry is permission for the defect. | F1210. `python3 tools/stack-depth-gate.py <elf> --arch x86_64 --fail 13000 --show-path`, before and after the split, frame breakdown as above. The branch was pushed with `SKIP_STACK_GATE=1`; the gate is already red on `main` for 36 f2fs paths. | unowned |
| OPEN | INFRA | high | **A lane's git worktree can be removed out from under it mid-task, and its branch rebased and pushed by another party.** During F1210 the worktree `/home/nd/oxide/kernel-F1210` was deleted while the lane was editing in it; the lane's commit was separately rebased onto a newer `origin/main` (`e76bcf3bb` -> `273a7f55b`) and pushed by someone else after the lane's own push failed on `stack-gate`. A second, lane-created worktree (`kernel-F1210-base`, holding a merge-base build for a baseline measurement) was deleted mid-build a few minutes later. Committed work survived intact and correctly authored; UNCOMMITTED work did not, and two measurements in flight were lost. CLAUDE.md already says a worktree belongs to its lane owner and may be removed only after the owner has finished — this is that rule being broken by tooling rather than by an agent. Consequence for every lane: commit early and often, because the working tree is not durable storage, and re-check `git rev-parse HEAD` before trusting any measurement taken earlier in the session. | F1210. `git reflog` shows `rebase (start): checkout origin/main` that this lane never ran; `cd` into both worktree paths returns `No such file or directory` while `git worktree list` had listed them minutes before. | unowned |

| FIXED F1218 | DEFECT | med | **f2fs `/sys/fs/f2fs/features/` withheld SEVEN features this build fully implements**, sending any tool that reads the directory away from working functionality: `encryption`, `test_dummy_encryption_v2`, `encrypted_casefold`, `block_zoned`, `atomic_write`, `pin_file`, `packed_ssa`. | RETRACTED F1218 | -- | -- | **"f2fs advertises `FEATURE_PACKED_SSA` but no reader implements the packed-SSA layout" is WRONG, and the fix an audit asked for would have removed conforming behaviour.** The feature fixes the summary block at 4096 bytes instead of taking the volume's block size. Both this build and the reference refuse any superblock whose stated block size is not 4096 before the feature word is consulted, so the two sizes are always equal: one summary block covers one segment, and the entry count, journal offset, journal length and footer offset are the same numbers either way. The ordinary summary reader IS the packed reader at the only block size a volume may state. | F1218. | FIXED F1218 | MISSING | high | **f2fs's three unbounded in-memory caches had no reclaim caller at all: nothing outside their own tests ever ran a shrink pass.** `extent::cache::Caches::shrink`/`shrink_default` and `freenid::state::shrink` were complete, tested and unreachable, so the read extent cache, the block-age extent cache and the free-node-id cache grew for the life of a mount and the only thing that ever shrank them was unmounting. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **f2fs's root block reserve is denied to EVERY caller, so `reserve_root=` reserves space nobody can ever use, and `resuid=`/`resgid=` are parsed and never consulted.** `volume/write.rs::volume_has_room` and `volume/space.rs::space` subtract `opts.reserve_root` unconditionally. The reference subtracts it only when the caller fails `__allow_reserved_root`, which passes on ANY of: no inode; a quota-file inode; `current_fsuid()` equal to `s_resuid`; `s_resgid` not the root group and the caller in that group; or `CAP_SYS_RESOURCE` where the call site permits it. The whole point of the reserve is that a privileged writer can still land a block on a full volume; here the emergency space is unreachable and a full volume is equally full for root. **Blocked on plumbing outside this crate:** the check needs the CALLER's fsuid, group set and `CAP_SYS_RESOURCE` at the block-allocation point, and `Volume` has no credential -- f2fs does not depend on `sched`, and the only way to a current cred (`cur.creds.to_vfs_cred`) lives in `syscalls`. The reference does not thread a cred either; it reads `current_fsuid()` at the check. So the fix is a probe hook installed from the layer that owns credentials, in the shape `vfs::quota::auth::set_quota_sys_resource_hook` already uses, with the decision itself an ungated function taking a caller record. Deliberately NOT half-built: an unwired decision function is the machinery-without-callers class this lane spent its time removing. | F1218. Reference `__allow_reserved_root` and `get_available_block_count`, Linux 7.2.0-rc4, whose subtraction is guarded by `test_opt(sbi, RESERVE_ROOT) && !__allow_reserved_root(...)`. Here: `volume/write.rs:132` and `volume/space.rs:41` have no cred term at all. `tests/nodeleak.rs:81` and `tests/pagewrite.rs:206` pin the current unconditional behaviour and would need updating with the fix. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **f2fs parses `checkpoint_merge`/`nocheckpoint_merge` and both spellings behave identically**, because there is no checkpoint thread to merge anything: `F2fs::checkpoint` is called synchronously by every caller (`mount/sb.rs::sync_fs`, `mount/write.rs::sync`, `mount/remount.rs`, `put_super`). The reference runs a dedicated kthread whose requests arrive on an llist; the thread drains all queued requests, performs exactly ONE checkpoint write, and completes every waiter with that one result -- so N concurrent `fsync`-driven `CP_SYNC` requests cost one disk checkpoint instead of N. Without it a heavy `fsync` workload pays a full checkpoint per caller. The reference also bypasses the thread for a non-`CP_SYNC` reason and for the umounting task itself, both of which must be reproduced. Its `ckpt_thread_ioprio` sysfs attribute (format `"rt,<level>"`/`"be,<level>"`, applied live) is absent for the same reason and is part of the same work. | F1218. Reference `f2fs_issue_checkpoint`, `issue_checkpoint_thread`, `__checkpoint_and_complete_reqs`, `ckpt_req_control`, Linux 7.2.0-rc4. Here `opts.checkpoint_merge` is read only by `opts/show.rs:95` and `opts/facts.rs:121`; nothing in `mount/` or `checkpoint/` consults it. The thread infrastructure to build on exists (`bg/run/kernel.rs` spawns the cleaner and the discard thread the same way). | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **f2fs parses `memory=low` and nothing acts on it, and the reference's effect is NOT the cache sizing an audit assumed -- it is decompression allocation timing.** Both of the reference's only two consumers concern compressed reads: `allow_memalloc_for_decomp` returns `pre_alloc ^ low_mem`, so normal mode allocates the decompression working set eagerly when the read context is created and low mode defers it to just before decompression runs; and the read-completion path runs a decompress-only step inline in normal mode but always defers it to a workqueue in low mode. Neither touches NAT/SIT caches, extent-cache limits or any shrinker. Here `compress/decompress.rs::decompress_cluster` allocates its output buffer at the moment it decompresses and drops it immediately, and the read path is synchronous with no context living across an in-flight request and no post-read workqueue -- so this build already behaves as `memory=low` unconditionally, and it is `memory=normal` that diverges by never pre-allocating. The work that makes the option real is the asynchronous compressed-read path, not a memory knob. | F1218. Reference `allow_memalloc_for_decomp`, `f2fs_prepare_decomp_mem`/`f2fs_release_decomp_mem` call sites in `f2fs_alloc_dic` and the decompress step, and the `f2fs_low_mem_mode` test in the bio end-read dispatch, Linux 7.2.0-rc4; no other consumer of `memory_mode` exists there. Here `opts.memory` is read only by `opts/show.rs:49-50` and `opts/facts.rs:116`. | unowned |

Every `CAP_SYS_RESOURCE` holder is therefore treated as unprivileged by quota enforcement. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **`make stack-gate` is RED on clean `main` with 52 static call paths over budget, and f2fs owns nearly all of them**, so every f2fs lane must bypass the pre-push stack gate and no lane can tell its own regression from the standing failure. Measured paths reach 19568 B against a 16384 B kernel stack -- i.e. past the whole stack, not merely past the 13000 B fail line -- so these are real overflow candidates, the class that produced the guard-page campaign. Worst offenders: `syscalls::fsmount_common::registry::register_filesystems::f2fs_fill` 19568 B, `F2fs::open_line` 18864 B, `F2fs::open_with` 17888 B, `Volume::mount_devices` 17120 B, `syscalls::ioctl::f2fs::handle_f2fs_ioctl` 16928 B, `f2fs::ioctl::exec::exec` 16080 B, and the four inode operations `mknod`/`mkdir`/`create`/`symlink` at ~15950-16000 B each. The mount chain is one summing call path and wants splitting so the big frames overlap, or the superblock and checkpoint buffers moving off-stack. `tools/stack-depth-allow-x86_64.txt` holds 31 entries and NOT ONE is f2fs, so nothing here is a waived deviation -- the gate has simply never been green since f2fs grew. | F1218, measured rather than inferred. Probe worktree at `origin/main` `1eea01d86`: `make stack-gate-x86` -> `FAIL - 52 static path(s) reach >= 13000 B`, 116 f2fs mentions. Same command on this branch -> 36 paths. So the failure predates the branch and the branch does not widen it; F1218 pushed with `SKIP_STACK_GATE=1`. The first probe attempt was void and is recorded so it is not repeated: overriding `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` breaks xtask's snapshot copy (`snapshot copy ... failed: No such file or directory`), so a probe worktree must use its own `target/`. | unowned |

## Memory / MM

| FIXED B2233 | DEFECT | high | **The reclaim LRU lock was taken plainly on a path that also runs in interrupt context, deadlocking the CPU against itself.** `free_one_frame` unlinks a page from its LRU on the way to the buddy free list, and pages are freed from interrupt context too, so an interrupt landing on the CPU that already held the lock spun for it forever with interrupts masked and no tick left to break it. The buddy lock beside it has masked interrupts since it was written, for the identical reason, and the reference unlinks from the LRU under an interrupt-safe lock on exactly this path. Now `lock_irqsave`. | B2233. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **A sleeping lock serializes rmap and mapcount transitions, so the unmap / COW / address-space-teardown path sleeps while holding spinlocks.** `rmap_aware_dec_and_maybe_free` takes the PMM page lock around the mapcount check and rmap detach, and `rmap.rs` takes it again in every `set_/clear_/get_` rmap helper. The reference takes NO folio lock on any of those paths: `zap_pte_range` runs under the page-table spinlock and calls the rmap-removal helper directly (zero folio-lock acquisitions in that function), mapcount is an atomic, and the rmap edge is protected by the anon-vma/i_mmap rwsem taken in process context. The folio lock there is a sleeping lock — its first statement is `might_sleep()` — reserved for I/O serialization. Ours forces it into a context that cannot sleep. | B2233. | FIXED B2234 | DEFECT | high | **`replace_mm` released the departing address space while holding `mm_pin_lock`, so `execve` slept under a spinlock.** The teardown attached to the last `AddressSpace` reference walks the whole page table and releases every leaf through the sleeping page-lock path; running that inside the pinned section made every `execve` a sleep-under-spinlock. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **Address-space teardown is attached to a refcount drop, so it inherits whatever lock context the last drop happens in.** `install_teardown` registers `as_teardown` as the `AddressSpace` drop hook; it walks the whole page table, releases every leaf through the sleeping rmap path, and frees the tables. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **`mremap` relocates a mapping by COPYING ITS BYTES; the reference relocates its PAGE TABLE ENTRIES.** `move_vma`/`move_page_tables`/`move_ptes` unlink the source's PTEs and re-link them under the destination's tables, so a move is O(page tables) and transfers no data at all. `mremap_full` instead reads the source and writes the destination through user addresses, which (a) costs O(bytes), (b) MATERIALISES every page of the source range — a sparse mapping arrives at the destination fully populated, where the reference moves only the entries that exist — and (c) is the reason the operation has any user access to lose in the first place. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **No lock is held across `mremap`, where the reference holds the mm write lock for the whole operation.** `mmap_write_lock_killable(mm)` is taken by the reference's syscall entry and released only at the end, with `vma_start_write` on the source on top; here `find_vma`, `mmap_preserving_prot` and `munmap` each take and RELEASE `AddressSpace::vmas` independently, so the VMA snapshot the checks approved is already stale when the transfer runs. ## Process / exec / signals

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

| OPEN | DEFECT | low | **A refused dynamic domain transition answers `EACCES` where the reference answers `EPERM`, and skips the bounded-transition test entirely.** Writing `/proc/self/attr/current` from a multi-threaded process is refused here outright; the reference first asks whether the new domain is BOUNDED by the old one and permits the write when it is, refusing with `EPERM` when it is not. So a legitimate bounded dyntransition is refused, and the errno a caller sees is the wrong one either way. | F1180. `crates/kernel/sched/src/selinux_label/attr.rs` returns `Errno::Eacces` on the multi-threaded path; no bounded-transition check exists in `crates/kernel/selinux` — `Type::bounds` is read only by the access-vector bounds masking. | unowned |
| IN-PROGRESS B2208-exec-stack-population | DEFECT | blocker | `execve` maps its initial stack lazily, then writes argv/envp/auxv through raw kernel pointers. A first stack-page fault arrives at CPL=0; any resolver failure is a fatal kernel fault instead of an `execve` error, leaving systemd children dead before the display path starts. The initial-stack writer must first populate only the planned argument/vector page range in the destination mm, then write it without a fault; it must not materialize the entire stack rlimit. | Fresh B2207 Q35/KVM run: first structural fault RIP resolves to `elf_load::stack::build_user_stack` during the direct initial-stack store; user processes then SIGSEGV before virtio-gpu scanout. Boot-init and UMH already use explicit initial-stack population; `execve` is the missing caller. | B2208-exec-stack-population |
| FIXED B2030 | DEFECT | med | **`modify_ldt` does not IPI the other CPUs running the address space.** The reference's `install_ldt` calls `flush_ldt` on every CPU in `mm_cpumask`, so a sibling thread sees a new descriptor before the installing thread's syscall returns. This port had no general cross-CPU call, so a sibling converged only at its NEXT return to user mode; inside that window a selector above the sibling's loaded limit faults. **B2030 built the missing primitive** — a real per-(sender,target) call-descriptor queue on the machine's single cross-CPU vector (`cpu::call_fn`, `arch_irq::call_fn`, `hal::smp_call`) — and `sched::ldt::install_entry` now converges every CPU in the mm's cpumask, waiting, before the syscall returns. The TLB shootdown rides the same queue rather than keeping its own single-slot protocol, so there is one cross-CPU mechanism, as upstream has. | B2030. Ordering pinned by `sched::ldt::converge::tests` (5); queue protocol by `cpu::call_fn::tests` (22); positive controls: freeing before the converge, dropping the drain reversal, releasing a slot before its handler ran, and converging a fixed mask each turned the matching test RED. | Chris Watkins |
| FIXED B2030 | DEFECT | low | **An LDT is allocated at full size (64 KiB) and never reallocated**, where the reference sizes the allocation to the highest entry in use and swaps the table on each grow. The swap is what its IPI exists to make safe. **B2030 landed the cross-CPU call and with it the swap:** `LdtState::install` allocates `max(entry+1, nr_entries)` descriptors, copies the old table forward and hands the displaced one back inside a `#[must_use] LdtSwap` whose only release point is `release_after_converge`. `base` and `nr_entries` are now published under a sequence counter because a moving base makes them a pair, and `sched::ldt::apply` reads that pair and executes `lldt` inside ONE interrupts-off window, as the reference requires. | B2030. `the_table_is_sized_to_the_highest_entry_not_to_the_architectural_maximum`, `growing_the_table_moves_the_base_and_hands_back_the_old_one`, `the_published_base_and_entry_count_are_always_one_installs_pair`. | Chris Watkins |

Closing it needs a userspace probe that installs an entry with `modify_ldt(0x11, …)`, loads it into `%ds`, and reads back through it. | B2024. `hal_x86_64::ldt` tests cover the encodings; the smoke boot proves only that the code does not break the boot. | unowned |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | **No booted kernel has run a cross-CPU LDT reload.** B2030's converge call is exercised by the queue's hosted tests and compiles into both arch builds, and the SMP=2 boot smoke proves the shared vector still carries TLB shootdown, but nothing in a Fedora boot calls `modify_ldt`, so `CallKind::LdtReload` has never been queued on real hardware. ## Drivers / devices

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

| OPEN | MISSING | med | **A PCR extend authorises with an empty password, not an HMAC session.** The reference has both paths: its integrity-protected extend wraps the command in an HMAC session derived through a salted P-256 ECDH exchange, and its `disable_pcr_integrity` path authorises with an empty password and is otherwise byte-identical. This implements the password path — a supported upstream branch, not a shortcut — because the session layer needs KDFa, HMAC and P-256 wired into `crypt`, which has none of the three. Without it a local attacker who can reach the chip's bus could forge an extend. | F1191. `tpm::device` 8 tests; the command image is asserted on the wire. | unowned |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | **An IMA extend the chip refuses is counted but not audited.** The reference reports it as an audit record carrying `TPM_error(n)` with the entry still added; here the entry is still added and a `tpm_failures` counter rises, because no audit path is wired from IMA yet. The counter exists so a chip refusing every extend cannot produce a log indistinguishable from an anchored one, but the reference's mechanism is the audit record. | F1191. `ima::list::tests::a_chip_that_refuses_the_extend_keeps_the_record_and_counts_the_failure`. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | high | **x86_64 boots on this box stop around 43 s with a preempt-count underflow in `irq_exit`, then `scheduling while atomic`.** `[PREEMPT-LEAK] irq_exit underflow cpu=1 entry_depth=1 preempt_count=0x…fe02 (HARDIRQ field already clear: the sub borrows into …)` immediately followed by `[BUG] scheduling while atomic: preempt_count=0xfffffe01 in_interrupt=1`: the count has wrapped negative, so every later `schedule()` from that CPU is refused and the guest goes silent. **It is intermittent, about one boot in two**: the control's first attempt hit it and its second booted clean in 48 s, and the same pattern shows across six runs at `-smp 1` and `-smp 2`. A gate whose per-attempt timeout is long therefore burns its whole budget on one frozen guest and never reaches the retry that would have passed. **The control rules V4L2 out as the trigger**: it reproduces on a plain `make smoke-x86` with no probe injected into the root and no camera opened by anything, and the earlier probe-carrying runs froze BEFORE their unit started. The same kernel and the same probe capture frames on aarch64, 3/3. Two earlier captures of the same stop name `NvmeBlk::drain_completions` and `VecDeque<Arc<Task>>::truncate` at `rflags=0x2`, both consistent with a CPU that can no longer reschedule. With a 240-second per-attempt timeout the V4L2 gate reaches a clean attempt and passes on x86 in 48 s, so the gate is usable; the underlying fault is not fixed. Not yet established: whether it predates this branch — the run that would say so is a plain `make smoke-x86` on `origin/main`, which has not been done. Worth pairing with the recorded uniprocessor-wedge row above, which describes a stop at the same phase of boot from a different angle. | F1179. The control's log carries the `[PREEMPT-LEAK]`/`[BUG]` pair on its first attempt and PASSes on its second in 48 s. `scratch/evidence/v4l2-{x86,arm}-acceptance.log` are full three-frame captures on the same commit, from the attempts that did not freeze. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **The virtual camera delivers ten frames a second when it negotiated thirty.** The acceptance capture's three frames are stamped 57.261492, 57.361644 and 57.461814 — 100 ms apart, three times the 33.3 ms period the device reported through `G_PARM` and paced its own `take_due` on. The producer is registered at a 16.6 ms interval, so the shortfall is not its own arithmetic; 100 ms is exactly the interval every other periodic timer in the tree uses, which points at the timer thread's own cadence rather than at the registration. An application that paces on the reported frame interval will run three times slower than it expects and will not be told why. | F1179. `scratch/evidence/v4l2-arm-acceptance.log`: `frame index=0 ... ts=57.261492`, `index=1 ... ts=57.361644`, `index=2 ... ts=57.461814`; `drv_vivid::tick::TICK_NS` is `1_000_000_000 / 60`; `drv_vivid::device::period_ns(1/30)` is 33_333_333 and is hosted-tested. | unowned |

| FIXED B2238 | DEFECT | med | **The legacy cursor ioctl collapsed three distinct refusals into `EINVAL`.** A card with no cursor plane must refuse an image request with `ENXIO` and a move with `EFAULT`, and an unknown CRTC id is `ENOENT` — three different answers a compositor deciding whether to software-composite its pointer can tell apart. All three were `EINVAL`, which reads as a malformed request not worth retrying. A request carrying `BO|MOVE` also performed the image and silently dropped the move, and a move was refused unless an image had already been published, where the reference calls the move entry point regardless. Root cause was the ops table typing both cursor entry points as `fn -> bool`, which cannot distinguish an absent entry point from a present one that failed; they are now `Option<fn>`, the reference's null-function-pointer shape, and the two cards that never supported a cursor pass `None` instead of a stub that only ever says no. | B2238. `drm::cursor_plan` (14 hosted tests). | FIXED F851 | DEFECT | med | virtio-blk's completion poll drained the SAME virtqueue the completion interrupt drives, so a polled ring saved the wait but not the interrupt. Closed by giving the driver a real interrupt-free poll queue: `VIRTIO_BLK_F_MQ` is negotiated, `num_queues` is read from device config, and when the device offers a queue to spare the driver programs a second request queue at the tail of the array with NO MSI-X vector bound and `VRING_AVAIL_F_NO_INTERRUPT` set in its driver area. The whole ring shadow is per-queue (`BlkQueue` owns the resource, the `RingShadow` and the lock), and the two drain contexts now PARTITION the queues: the completion softirq drains only queues whose completions the device signals, a poll drains only the interrupt-free ones. A transfer submitted through the direct path is stamped `polled` and issued on the poll queue; `can_poll` is true only when such a queue exists, so a single-queue device reports no poll capability rather than promising a saving it cannot make. The QEMU launchers pass `num-queues=2` on every virtio-blk device, since one queue is the default and the negotiation would otherwise find nothing to negotiate. | F851. 15 new hosted tests across `drv-virtio-blk` (33→49) and `virtio` (58→61) plus 2 in `block` (85→87): queue-count arithmetic, the tail placement, the never-take-the-last-queue rule, the `avail.flags` suppression store, the softirq/poll partition, the capability gate and the direct path's `polled` stamp. Positive controls, each RED then restored GREEN: cursor not advancing (2 RED), poll queue allowed to take the last default queue (3 RED), suppression store removed (1 RED), `can_poll` hardcoded true (1 RED), softirq draining every queue (1 RED), direct path not stamping `polled` (1 RED), optional queue withheld from the child (1 RED), poll queue given a completion handler (1 RED). Boot evidence, both arches and both disks: `[INFO]  virtio-blk poll queue idx=1 of 2 msix=000000000000ffff avail_flags=0000000000000001` — the queue count and the bound vector read back out of the device's own configuration space, the suppression bit read back out of the driver area. `ffff` is the no-vector sentinel: the device has nothing to raise for that queue. | virtio-blk lane |

The cursor arithmetic now lives in ungated functions in `modern/drain.rs` — `drain_admitted` (no ring, no HHDM, or a synchronous turn-holder) and `claim_next_used` (claim one entry, advance the cursor) — with the walker calling them; the split-ring offsets it loads from are named constants covered by their own test. | F851. `modern::drain::tests`: `used_ring_offsets_match_the_split_ring_layout`, `a_drain_is_refused_without_a_ring_or_against_a_synchronous_owner`, `two_interleaved_drains_claim_each_used_entry_exactly_once` (counted as a MULTISET, so a double claim cannot be deduped away), `a_drained_ring_claims_nothing`, `the_cursor_wraps_with_the_free_running_used_index`. Positive control: deleting the cursor advance turns 2 of them RED; restored GREEN. | — |
