## Kernel core

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | MISSING | high | **The LSM framework has no consumer, so the hazard it was written to remove is still live.** `crates/kernel/lsm` centralises which security modules run and in what order, which slot of an object each owns, and what "ask every module" means — its own header records why: before it, each module wired its own call sites by hand, so a check point that consulted one module and not another was a SILENT GRANT with nothing red. Its builtin order is `landlock,selinux`, both of which are live in this kernel, so this is a refactor of running code and not speculative surface. Until `security`, `selinux` and `landlock` are routed through it, every call site is still individually responsible for remembering to ask both. | `grep -rln '^lsm *=' --include=Cargo.toml crates/` returns nothing: no crate depends on it. `crates/kernel/lsm/src/modules.rs::BUILTIN_ORDER` is `"landlock,selinux"`. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | high | **There is no TPM character device, so no userspace can reach the chip.** The driver landed with both wire transports and a `Chip` that marshals and transmits real commands, but nothing probes a chip and nothing publishes `/dev/tpm0` or `/dev/tpmrm0`. Everything a Fedora userspace uses a TPM for is therefore unavailable: `systemd-cryptenroll` and `systemd-cryptsetup` TPM2 disk unlock, `systemd-pcrphase`/`pcrlock`, tpm2-tss and tpm2-tools, clevis. The missing pieces are a probe and a chardev, not the protocol. | F1191 merged the driver; `grep -rn tpm crates/kernel/devfs/src crates/drivers/` returns nothing. `crates/kernel/tpm/src/{tis,crb}.rs` carry the transports. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | high | **IMA measures nothing: it has no hooks and no securityfs surface.** The measurement list, template digests and the extend path into a real chip are implemented and tested, and `ima` depends on `tpm` — but no crate depends on `ima`, so nothing measures at exec, mmap or open, nothing appraises a signature, and `/sys/kernel/security/ima/` does not exist. A measured boot is therefore not merely unattested, it is not attempted. Blocked on the two rows above: it needs LSM hooks to be called from, and it needs the chip to be reachable. | `grep -rln '^ima *=' --include=Cargo.toml crates/` returns nothing. `crates/kernel/ima` has 170 passing tests and no caller. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **AppArmor and TEE do not exist.** Branch `F1191-ima-tpm-tee-apparmor` planned IMA, TPM, TEE and AppArmor as one group; only IMA and TPM were built. AppArmor is a security module the LSM framework would carry alongside SELinux and Landlock, and a Fedora userspace expects one of the two to be present. There is no `crates/kernel/apparmor` and no `crates/kernel/tee`. | `ls crates/kernel/{apparmor,tee}` — both absent. Recorded so the branch name is not mistaken for the delivered scope. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **The TPM extend authorises with an empty password rather than an HMAC session.** Both are real upstream paths — the reference's own no-integrity branch is byte-identical apart from the auth area — so this is a weaker choice rather than an invented one. The session variant needs KDFa, HMAC and P-256 key exchange. Also: IMA counts a refused extend in `tpm_failures` but does not audit it, where the reference emits an audit record naming the failure. | F1191. `crates/kernel/tpm/src/device.rs` header records the choice; `ima::MeasurementList::tpm_failures`. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **One aarch64 boot lost its root filesystem mid-sysinit: `mkdirat /var/lib/systemd/pstore` answered EIO, five udev workers were killed by SIGBUS, and `systemd-zram-setup@zram0` exited 1.** SIGBUS on a userspace fault is the mmap'd-page-cannot-be-faulted answer, and EIO on a plain `mkdirat` says the same thing from the syscall side, so both point at the block/ext4 read path rather than at any one service. Seen ONCE, in the only aarch64 boot so far that got past journald, and with an x86 image build running concurrently on the same host — so it is not established whether it is a kernel defect or host contention. Recorded because the shape (a filesystem that starts answering EIO under load) is one nothing else in the tree would catch. | B2244 arm probe boot, guest log kept at `scratch/evidence/b2244/arm-probe-3.log`: `[55.195] [NAMEI] mkdirat path="/var/lib/systemd/pstore" err=5`, `[55.617] systemd-udevd[81]: 0000:00:05.0: Worker [93] terminated by signal 7 (BUS).` and four more, `[55.859] systemd-zram-setup@zram0.service: Main process exited, code=exited, status=1/FAILURE`. Three other aarch64 boots of the same image in the same session show none of it. | unowned |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | **`/proc/<pid>/wchan` answers `0`, `/proc/<pid>/syscall` answers `running`, and `/proc/<pid>/stack` is always empty, for a task that is sleeping.** All three files exist and none of them answers: the reference names the kernel function a blocked task is parked in and, for `syscall`, prints the syscall number, its six arguments, the stack pointer and the instruction pointer, or `running` only when the task really is on a CPU. Every "where is this process stuck" probe on this kernel — the one thing those files are for — therefore returns nothing usable, and a wedge has to be chased with a task dump instead. | B2237, extended B2244 (`procfs::live::pid_dir` binds `stack` to `pc_empty`, so the third file is a stub by construction). In-guest, against two tasks both reporting `State: S (sleeping)` in `/proc/<pid>/status`: `PS 312 0 | running` and `PS 295 0 | running`, where the fields are `wchan` then `syscall`. | unowned |
| IN-PROGRESS F1180 | DEFECT | high | **The security server is held under a SPINLOCK across work that sleeps, so a boot with a policy loaded storms `scheduling while atomic`.** The engine allocates on the check path — SID-table growth, context rendering, cache insertion — and an allocation here reaches something that parks. Preemption is disabled by the lock, so the scheduler reports the violation on every check and the boot never completes. Invisible to every hosted test: the lock, the preempt counter and the block layer only exist on the kernel target, which is exactly why this needed the boot. | F1180 boot, aarch64, all 3 smoke attempts identical: `[BUG] scheduling while atomic: preempt_count=0000000000000002 held_lock_rank=150 held=[150@crates/kernel/selinux-runtime/src/lib.rs:60] caller=crates/kernel/sched/src/live/wait_event.rs:168`, repeating from 46.8s until the gate gave up. The module itself comes up correctly first: `[6.185] [INFO] selinux: enabled, awaiting policy`. | F1180 |
| OPEN | INFRA | high | **Whether this branch boots to the login marker is UNDETERMINED: every boot attempt tonight ran against a sibling lane's concurrent boots.** Four attempts on two arches all timed out at their deadline with systemd spinning in `sched_yield`, but `ps` showed worktree `kernel-F1179` running its own x86 AND aarch64 boots for the whole window, which is exactly the contention CLAUDE.md warns produces false failures. A control boot of the same kernel with `selinux=0` ALSO timed out, under the same contention, so it distinguishes nothing either. What the boots DID establish, because contention cannot suppress it, is the absence of the atomic storm above. This needs one uncontended boot on an idle box before anything is concluded about the login marker. | F1180. `ps` during the window: `qemu-system-x86_64 -pidfile /home/nd/oxide/kernel-F1179/...` and `qemu-system-aarch64 -pidfile /home/nd/oxide/kernel-F1179/...` plus `boot-smoke.sh arm 900` / `x86 900`. Task dump at the timeout: `1 3235774466 systemd R ... sched_yiel 3056186724 561082`. | F1180 |
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **`/sys/fs/selinux/status` is a read-only file, not the mappable page userspace expects.** The reference publishes a small page that libselinux maps once and then polls in userspace, so a process learns about an enforcement change or a policy reload without a syscall per check. Ours serves the same five fields through an ordinary read, so every consumer falls back to its polling path — correct answers, at a syscall each. | F1180. `crates/kernel/selinuxfs/src/nodes/misc.rs` serves `status` as a five-word little-endian read; no `mmap` operation is installed on the inode. | unowned |
| OPEN | INFRA | low | **`selinuxfs` declares its inode-number region locally instead of in the shared table.** `crates/kernel/selinuxfs` carries its own `Region::new("selinuxfs", 0x7B00_0000, 0x7B0F_FFFF)` with a compile-time disjointness assertion against `vfs::pseudo_ino::REGIONS`, rather than being a row in that table. The assertion means an overlap cannot ship, but the table is supposed to be the one place a region is claimed, and a second place to look is how two filesystems end up sharing a range. | F1180. `crates/kernel/selinuxfs/src/lib.rs` region declaration versus `crates/kernel/vfs/src/pseudo_ino.rs` `REGIONS`. Related pre-existing fact found while checking: FSID `0x0102_1994_0000_0006` is already used by BOTH `tracefs` and `devpts`. | unowned |
| OPEN | MISSING | high | **There is no LSM hook dispatch: every security module wires its own call sites by hand.** The only registry-shaped thing in the tree is `security::bpf_lsm`, which has exactly ONE hook (`FileOpen`) and one caller. Landlock reaches the VFS through a bespoke glue module, capability checks live in `sched`, seccomp in `security`, and SELinux (F1180) now adds a fourth pattern. The reference dispatches every module through one hook table, which is what makes "both modules must agree" a property of the framework rather than of each call site remembering to ask both. The ordinary relocation was accidentally immune because it runs the device shutdown, which takes the port out of queue mode as a side effect. | F854. Measured: a crash boot's log stopped mid-word right after the interrupt controller was cleared, while the ORDINARY relocation on the same guest and the same harness printed every line of the same code path (`tools/kexec-crash-smoke.sh x86 600 reboot` is that control). | F854 |

| IN-PROGRESS B2204-x86-cr2-entry | DEFECT | CRITICAL | **PMM used a raw `LOCKED`-bit spin for blocking page acquisition.** Rmap release and ext4 cached-page I/O could repeatedly spin with preemption disabled when another task owned the page, starving the owner on one CPU. The current correction uses a bounded page wait table and a per-page waiter marker, clearing the lock bit before wake. | Retained `target/boot-logs/B2204-x86-drm-runtime/final.log`: at guest 22.746, `systemd-remount` stayed at `0xffffffff805c8f02` with `preempt_count=0x10001`; disassembly identified the retry after a failed atomic `LOCKED` acquisition. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **A uniprocessor x86_64 boot wedges in early userspace: a task spins forever in a kernel spinlock whose owner is not running.** Measured with the boot gate's own liveness probe on one prebuilt image: `-smp 1` answers 2/5 boots, `-smp 2` answers 3/3. A wedged guest stops at the same place every time — right after journald's runtime-journal flush, while udev workers and systemd are opening devices — and never switches task again (`[WATCHDOG] soft lockup: no reschedule for 10s`, then `no-progress: 0 context switches for 40s`, with `switches=` identical in both dumps). Two independent captures name the stall site exactly: a `sys_openat` of a console device parked in `Spinlock::lock_irqsave::<PortInner<VtConsoleDriver>, Tty>` from `TtyStruct::open_inner`, and a `block::registry::open_by_dev` parked in `Spinlock::lock::<Vec<Arc<Disk>>, Devices>`. The owner is off-CPU in both: with one CPU nothing can run it, and the spinner holds interrupts masked so no tick, no wakeup and no reschedule can reach it. A second CPU picks the owner up, which is why every gate in the tree — all of which run `-smp 2` — passes. Not yet root-caused: the specific point at which an owner gives up the CPU while holding the lock is unidentified. Ruled out with evidence, so the next lane need not retry them: cross-CPU call/TLB-shootdown wait (`call_function_many` returns immediately when `online_count() <= 1`, so no shootdown is ever issued); a reschedule taken from `preempt_enable`/`preempt_check_resched` with interrupts masked (fixed on this branch and measured — post-fix `-smp 1` is 1/5, unchanged within the sample). | B2047. Captures under `scratch/evidence/` are reproducible with `OXIDE_SMP=1 ./tools/boot-smoke.sh x86`; a live guest attached with gdb shows `#3 Spinlock::lock_irqsave<PortInner<VtConsoleDriver>,Tty> #4 TtyStruct::open_inner #6 vt_console::on_open_file #8 vfs::file::open::open_file_at #13 sys_openat`, and QMP register sampling pins RIP inside that one `cmpxchg`/`pause` loop across every sample. `make smoke-up-x86` is the gate that fails on it. | — |
| OPEN | DEFECT | CRITICAL | **Block-device lifecycle synchronization has the wrong primitive and is a P0 remediation.** The registry holds a general spinlock while admitting and releasing VFS openers, holders, and destructive lifecycle operations. Those are process-context operations that can contend behind a preempted or I/O-blocked owner; they must not spin. The completion-side in-flight accounting is legitimately softirq-shared and must remain separately non-sleeping. Required shape: a sleepable lifecycle/open lock for publication, open/close, holder and quiesce serialization; a distinct BH-safe short admission lock/counter for in-flight I/O. Do not convert the existing combined state lock wholesale, and do not accept a timer backstop as a fix. | B2047 audit, `block::registry::{open_by_dev,close_by_dev,claim,release,try_quiesce}` and the recorded `udev` `openat` spin. | active |

| FIXED F859 | DEFECT | high | **An aarch64 `kexec_file_load(2)` relocation ran and the relocated kernel died in device probe before userspace** — a data abort on a pointer full of `0xc2` bytes inside `__insert_inode_hash` under `bdev_add` under `virtblk_probe`. Cause: this kernel's interrupt controller keeps reading its LPI configuration table and writing its pending table across the jump, and NOTHING told the next kernel. That kernel found LPIs still enabled, adopted the tables the registers pointed at, and rewrote the configuration table over its whole 64 KiB granule — while this kernel had allocated only 16 KiB (one byte per LPI at 14 ID bits), and while the range was in neither the placement map's exclusions nor the handover tree's reservations. The faulting bucket sat at `0xbf33e9a0`, `0x69a0` into the table at `0xbf338000` — past the 16 KiB allocated, inside the 64 KiB rewritten — and `0xc2` is an LPI configuration byte, which is why the value looked like poison and the victim looked unrelated. Fixed on both halves: the tables are allocated at the granule an adopting kernel assumes, and their extents are recorded once and used twice — cut from the placement map so no segment lands there, and carried into the handover tree so the new kernel's allocator never hands them out. NOTE for row below: which CPU runs the relocation decides whether the bug fires at all — the new kernel boots on that CPU's redistributor, and this kernel enables LPIs only on the boot CPU's, so a relocation from a secondary never takes the adopting path. The earlier "the parked secondary is ruled out as the differentiator" reading was wrong. | F859. `scratch/evidence/kexec-arm-file-load-userspace.log`: `kexec -s -l` rc=0, `kexec -e`, second kernel banner from `[0.000000]`, `Run /bin/sh as init process`, shell answers `NEWSH-42`; 2/2 runs reached userspace. The handover reservations are directly measured: the new kernel reports `163408K reserved` against `163280K` before, exactly the two 64 KiB tables. 11 hosted tests (`kexec::file_load::reserved`, `arm_image::handover`, `arm_image::assemble`, `arch_irq::gic_lpi_layout`, `firmware::memreserve`); positive controls — tree reservations dropped -> RED, placement subtraction disabled -> RED (incl. the end-to-end loader test), table sized at `1 << IDbits` -> RED; all restored -> GREEN. | F859 |

| FIXED F849 | MISSING | med | x86_64 kexec now stops the machine completely before relocating. Every local-vector-table entry is masked and the local APIC software-disabled, then the boot interrupt mode is restored — APIC re-enabled on the low spurious vector, the legacy pin delivering ExtINT and the NMI pin delivering NMI — so a kernel that expects legacy delivery before it programs its own routing gets it. The I/O APIC is CLEARED rather than merely masked: an entry is masked, any level assertion still in service retired, and the entry then flattened, because a line whose remote-IRR bit stays set can never be received by the next kernel. Entries delivering system-management interrupts are left to firmware. The stop order is now interrupt sources first, then IRQs off and the other CPUs halted, then the local APIC — an interrupt delivered to an APIC mid-teardown wedges some implementations. | F849. The encoding lives in the ungated `arch_irq::lapic_shutdown` (11 host tests) precisely because the module owning the local APIC is compiled only into the kernel target, so tests written inside it never build — see the phantom-test row below. Positive controls: error entry masked last -> 1 RED; boot mode leaving the APIC software-disabled -> 1 RED; virtual-wire entry left masked -> 2 RED; the NMI pin given ExtINT delivery -> 1 RED; the version field read from the wrong byte -> 1 RED; the clean pass writing a register the implementation lacks -> 1 RED; all restored -> GREEN. | — |

| FIXED F849 | DEFECT | med | The aarch64 identity map's reach was a hardcoded constant that assumed one translation-control value. A kernel configured for a narrower output size previously got a map built for addresses its hardware truncates, with nothing able to notice. PREMISE CORRECTION on the original row: recomputing per image does not by itself reach past 2^48 here. Doing that needs the large-physical-address table format and a wider output size, which is a kernel-wide translation change, not a kexec one — this kernel is 48-bit throughout. Kept as a separate row below. | F849. `kexec::machine::plan::{arm_idmap_limit,tcr_with_idmap_t0sz,ips_bits}`, 3 host tests. | FIXED F849 | DEFECT | low | The x86_64 trampoline left no descriptor table at entry, so an image that reloaded a segment register before installing its own would fault with no table to describe a handler. It now does both halves. On entry, while the running table is still live, it force-loads the kernel data selector into DS/ES/SS/FS/GS — the only thing that refreshes the hidden descriptors a segment register carries — so those descriptors hold known-good flat ring-0 segments across the window in which no table exists; nothing after that instruction may touch per-CPU state, since loading GS with a selector zeroes its base. Once the identity map is live it installs a flat four-entry table that travels inside the trampoline blob, so it lands in the control page, the one page guaranteed to sit outside every destination. | F849. `kexec::machine::plan::{GDT_ENTRY_*,GDT_LIMIT}`, 2 host tests decoding every descriptor field. Verified in the linked image, not inferred: the entry sequence disassembles to `cli` then five `mov %eax,%seg` with 0x30, and the table is 4 x 8 bytes at a 16-byte boundary inside the blob with a limit of 0x1f. Positive controls: the 64-bit entry losing its long-mode bit -> 1 RED; the data entry not writable -> 1 RED; the limit one byte too long -> 1 RED; restored -> GREEN. | — |

| FIXED F849 | MISSING | med | `crashkernel=` is parsed, reserved and consumed end to end, so a `KEXEC_ON_CRASH` load has somewhere legal to land and the panic path boots it. The grammar takes the plain `size[@offset]` form, range tables (`start-end:size`, open-topped, comma-chained), and the `,high` / `,low` / `,cma` suffixes, with binary suffixes and hex; placement is 16 MiB aligned, searches below the 32-bit boundary first (above it for `,high`) and falls back to the other window. The region is taken out of the page allocator before the first allocation. A crash image is staged ENTIRELY inside it: its control pages come from a bump walk over the region that steps over its own destinations, and its segments are written straight to their destinations at load time — there is nothing to relocate, and pages from the allocator would be memory the running kernel overwrites long before the panic. The panic handler tries the crash boot after the log snapshot and before the halt-or-restart branch, try-locking both the kexec lock and the slots because the panicking CPU may hold either. | F849. `kexec::crashk::{parse,place,shrink,boot,panic}` + `kexec::store::crash_kexec_now`; 55 + 5 host tests. Positive controls, all restored to GREEN: 16 defects reinstated across the grammar, the placement and the shrink each went RED; crash control pages taken from the allocator -> 3 RED; crash segments staged instead of written in place -> 3 RED; reserved pages handed back to the allocator on free -> 1 RED; a region control page allowed to sit on a destination -> 2 RED; the in-place load skipping its clear -> 1 RED; the crash boot ignoring the lock -> 1 RED; the crash boot leaking the lock on refusal -> 1 RED. TWO of these controls initially came back GREEN and the tests were rewritten until they could fail. | — |

The bit is live in the ABI on the platform class this kernel targets, where a load carrying it is accepted and the loaded image returns control to the kernel that started it — so the refusal is OURS. It stands only because nothing here can bring this kernel back: the contract needs the whole return path — user processes frozen, devices suspended, core system state saved on the way out and restored on the way in — and NONE of it exists (`crates/kernel/power` has no suspend path of any kind; grep for a process freeze or a device-suspend pass over the driver model returns nothing). Accepting the bit and ignoring it would turn 'resume afterwards' into 'never resume' silently, which is worse than an errno, so EINVAL is the right answer TODAY and the row stays open until the return path is built. | FIXED B2033 | MISSING | med | `IORING_SETUP_IOPOLL` was refused because "no backend here can be polled for finished I/O". That premise was TRUE and is now closed rather than argued with: `BlockDevice::poll_completions`/`can_poll` and `FileOps::iopoll`/`can_iopoll` give this kernel the reference's `f_op->iopoll` chain, virtio-blk implements it over its existing used-ring drain, and `IORING_SETUP_IOPOLL` is implemented on top — polled rings admit only the opcodes a poll can complete (EINVAL otherwise), a polled transfer must be direct and land on a pollable backend (EOPNOTSUPP otherwise), `RWF_HIPRI` is EINVAL on a ring that does not poll, and `IORING_ENTER_GETEVENTS` drives the backend poll instead of parking on a wait list nothing would signal. `HYBRID_IOPOLL` stays refused with its OWN reason now (it sleeps against a per-request service-time estimate this block layer does not keep), not "IOPOLL is refused" — see the separate row. | B2033. `io_uring_abi::iopoll` (18 hosted tests, ungated); `layout/tests.rs::every_setup_flag_is_either_implemented_or_refused` now records IOPOLL as implemented and would FAIL if it regressed to refused. | FIXED F841 | DEFECT | low | A polled io_uring transfer used to run INLINE and post its completion before anything could poll for it, so `IORING_ENTER_GETEVENTS` returned from the precheck and the poll loop never ran. Closed by building the missing mechanism rather than arguing the premise: `FileOps::submit_direct` (`vfs::file_ops::DirectIo`/`DirectSubmit`) is the reference's `-EIOCBQUEUED` arm, the block bridge implements it over `BlockDevice::submit`'s existing owned-request completion, and a transfer on a polled ring is now SUBMIT-THEN-POLL: prepared in the submitter (segments imported, write payload gathered), issued to the backend, and left with NO CQE until `io_uring_enter(GETEVENTS)` drives the poll and reaps it. The request is held by a STRONG `ctx->iopoll_list` on the ring, because nothing else holds a transfer the backend owns; the backend's completion owns only the result slot, never the request or user memory, so it is safe from any context and after the ring is gone. Exactly-once completion goes through the existing `IoReq::claim`. A read's bytes are scattered into the SUBMITTER's address space by page-table root, not through whichever task reaps. | F841. Hosted: `block::devbridge::tests::a_queued_direct_read_returns_without_completing` (submitted, `slot` still empty, device holds it, only `iopoll` completes it), `a_queued_direct_write_lands_and_a_later_read_sees_it`, `f_op_submit_direct_on_an_open_block_description_reaches_the_driver`; `block::direct::plan` (9 tests). Positive control: making the fixture's `submit` complete inline turned exactly those 3 deferral tests RED; restored GREEN. | — |
| FIXED F841 | MISSING | low | `IORING_SETUP_HYBRID_IOPOLL` is implemented. The estimate it sleeps against is the ring's own running MINIMUM of observed service times — the reference's `ctx->hybrid_poll_time`, not a block-layer statistic, which is what the old refusal got wrong about where the number lives. A queued transfer is stamped at issue, each poll pass sleeps for half the current estimate before polling (once per transfer, never once per pass), and folds `now - issued_at - slept` back in as a minimum. Minimum rather than average for the reference's stated reason: with backends of different speeds, sleeping longer than the fastest takes loses completions that were ready. | F841. `io_uring_abi::iopoll::{hybrid_sleep_ns,observe_runtime,hybrid_runtime}` + 6 hosted tests, including one that feeds the estimate through its own sleep 8 times and asserts it does not ratchet. `layout/tests.rs::every_setup_flag_is_either_implemented_or_refused` now records it as implemented and fails if it regresses. | — |
| FIXED B2049 | COVERAGE | low | Closed. The three orderings that were boot-only are pinned: claim asked LAST (a parked transfer is never claimed at all), release BEFORE post, and reap AFTER the poll that finished the transfer — including on the pass that gives up the processor, where a reap-first loop strands the completion. Driven end to end against a fixture backend that parks transfers and only finishes them on a poll, the same shape as the block layer's `QueuedDisk`. | B2049. 22 hosted tests in `io_uring_abi::iopoll::seq::tests`. | FIXED F848 | MISSING | med | `SQE128` / `SQE_MIXED` / `CQE_MIXED` are implemented (`CQE32` already was). The geometry carries both strides: a dedicated wide ring doubles the array it strides, a mixed ring keeps the narrow stride and spends TWO adjacent slots on a wide entry. A mixed ring shallower than two entries is EOVERFLOW at sizing. `IORING_OP_NOP128` is dispatched, so the submission-side flags have an operation that needs them. | F848. `io_uring_abi::{sqe_slot,cqe_slot,nop}` (28 hosted tests); `sqe128_doubles_the_submission_stride_and_the_array_it_sizes`, `a_mixed_ring_keeps_the_narrow_stride_and_the_narrow_size`. | FIXED F848 | MISSING | low | `NO_MMAP` / `REGISTERED_FD_ONLY` are implemented. A `NO_MMAP` ring pins the caller's pages at `p->cq_off.user_addr`/`p->sq_off.user_addr` for its whole life and uses them in place, zeroed first; the two fields now survive admission instead of being cleared. Such a ring is not mappable from its descriptor, and `REGISTERED_FD_ONLY` installs it into the calling task's registered-ring array and returns that index instead of spending a descriptor. Per-page access is sound because no ring object straddles a page, walked over every admitted geometry. | F848. `io_uring_abi::user_ring` (4 hosted tests incl. `no_ring_object_ever_straddles_a_page`); `caller_supplied_addresses_survive_admission_and_others_are_cleared`. | FIXED F848 | MISSING | low | `ATTACH_WQ` is implemented: a poll thread serves a SET of rings, a ring joins the thread of a ring in its own thread group, and each ring's share of a pass is capped once more than one is attached. A descriptor naming nothing is ENXIO, a non-ring EINVAL, a ring without a thread EINVAL, a dead thread ENXIO; one naming a thread in another thread group is not refused but gets a thread of its own, because that thread borrows another process's address space. The thread now exits with its LAST ring. | F848. `io_uring_abi::sqpoll::{attach_admit,ring_take,shares,sweep}` (17 new hosted tests). Positive control: dropping the dead-thread, cross-group and disabled-ring clauses turned 3 tests RED; restored GREEN. | F848 |
| FIXED F848 | MISSING | low | `SQ_REWIND` is implemented. A rewinding ring is an array userspace rewrites in place: every pass starts at slot zero, is bounded by the array and the caller's own `to_submit` rather than by the tail, and does not publish the head it reached. The submission engine carries its own cursor for the pass instead of reading and writing the shared head word per entry. | F848. `io_uring_abi::sq_cursor` (6 hosted tests). Positive control: treating a rewinding ring as an ordinary one turned 3 tests RED; restored GREEN. | F848 |

The reference reaps a polled ring from the poll thread itself. Fixed: a ring with outstanding transfers is work for the pass whether or not an entry is published, and the pass reaps before it submits. | B2049. `io_uring_abi::sqpoll::thread::tests::a_polled_ring_completes_without_its_submitter_entering` (three transfers, submitter never enters, all three complete). | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **`make frame-gate` is RED on `main`: one frame over the 8192 B ceiling, `register_filesystems::{closure#6}` at 17664 B.** The closure is the f2fs mount constructor, and the whole frame is `F2fs::open_with` inlined into it -- outlining `open_with` moves 20904 B onto its own frame and drops the closure to 4936 B. Both frame baselines (`tools/frame-size-baseline-{x86_64,aarch64}.txt`) are EMPTY, so this is not a tolerated waiver; the gate has simply been red. **Two hypotheses measured and DISPROVED, recorded so the next lane does not repeat them:** (1) the by-value copies of the 4504-byte `Volume` and 4576-byte `F2fs` -- boxing the volume (`Spinlock<Box<Volume<Medium>>>`, so only a pointer moves, which is the reference's shape: it kzallocs the equivalent state) changed the frame by EXACTLY ZERO, 17664 before and after, and 20904 for the isolated `open_with` both ways; (2) four 4096-byte stack objects -- the four `sub $0x1000` in the prologue are LLVM stack-clash PROBES, one per page of a 17608-byte frame, not four objects, and f2fs allocates every block buffer with `vec!`. `Volume::mount_devices`, which builds the whole 4504-byte volume, does not even reach the top-15 (<=4392 B), so the volume construction is not the cost. Outlining `open_members`, `zone_reports`, `medium`, `DevTable::scan` and `adopt_data_pages` also changed nothing. **RETRACTED, and the retraction is the finding -- see the aarch64 decoder rows below.** This row previously read: *the decisive datum is that `make frame-gate-arm` PASSES* -- zero frames over 8192 on aarch64, where the largest is 4176 and f2fs's biggest is `Volume::lookup` at 4032. The same source, the same `open_with`, produces 17664 B on x86_64 and under 4 KB on aarch64. So this is an x86_64 CODEGEN outcome -- an inlining decision plus x86's stack-slot allocation -- and not a data-shape problem in f2fs, which is why reshaping the data (the boxed volume) moved nothing. That conclusion was wrong. aarch64 carries the SAME frame -- 17728 B against x86's 17664 -- and the gate could not see it, because its aarch64 parser could not decode the shifted immediate every frame over 4095 bytes uses. So the two arches agree, the frame is intrinsic to `open_with` on both, and the x86-target-JSON lead below is dead. This is the `never state a conclusion a proxy cannot support` rule: a PASS from a gate that cannot fail was read as evidence about the code. Still unmeasured: `Arc::new_cyclic`, `Bg::new`, and the drop glue on `open_with`'s five `?` paths. One OBSERVATION, now DEAD as an explanation since both arches measure the same frame, kept only so nobody re-derives it: the x86 kernel target disables every SIMD register file (`-mmx,-sse,...,+soft-float`) while asking for `x86-64-v3`, and pins `frame-pointer: always` and inline stack probes; aarch64 disables its vector unit too but is otherwise plain `generic`. A backend with no vector registers to hold values in spills them, which is a plausible mechanism for a five-fold frame difference on identical source -- but it is a hypothesis, not a measurement, and the next lane should test it before acting on it. Cheapest next experiment: `mount_devices` returns `Result<Volume, Errno>` and `open_with` immediately writes `.map_err(errno_to_vfs)?`, which materialises the 4504-byte volume into a second `Result` slot before the `?` moves it into a third; four such slots is 18016 B, within noise of the frame. Returning the mapped error type directly, or taking the volume by out-parameter, would collapse them -- and would be measurable in one build. | B2248. `make frame-gate-x86` at `a9df29c93`; `nm -S` gives the closure 0x6d81 of code; prologue `sub $0x1000` x4 + `sub $0x4c8` = 17608 + 48 saved = 17656. Type sizes from `-Zprint-type-sizes` on `cargo test -p f2fs --release`. | unowned |

| FIXED B2248 | INFRA | blocker | **`make frame-gate-arm` was structurally INCAPABLE of failing on any large aarch64 frame, and had been passing a 30016-byte one while reporting the binary's largest as 4176.** aarch64's `sub` immediate field is 12 bits, so LLVM emits every stack reservation over 4095 bytes as a SHIFTED immediate -- `sub sp, sp, #0x7, lsl #12` is 28672 bytes. The gate's `ARM_SUB` regex stopped at the immediate and ignored the `, lsl #12` tail, so it did not merely miss those frames: it scored a 28672-byte reservation as **7 bytes**. Large frames over 4 KB are the only thing this gate exists to catch, and on aarch64 that is exactly the set carrying the tail it could not read. The x86 half was unaffected (x86 splits a big frame into page-sized probes the parser already summed), which is why the arch difference read as a codegen difference for most of this lane. Fixed by decoding the shift, with the real prologue as a self-test case. | B2248. Found by a positive control: injecting `[0u8; 12288]` into `F2fs::open_with` left `frame-gate-arm` GREEN at "largest 4176". Hand-decoding the prologue of `register_filesystems::{closure#6}` gives `stp ... #-96` + `sub sp,sp,#0x7,lsl #12` + `sub sp,sp,#0x4e0` = 30016. After the fix the gate reports 30016 with the control and 17728 without -- a delta of exactly 12288. `--self-test` now covers `arm_shifted_frame`; the pre-fix parser scores that case 1383 instead of 30016. | B2248-green-main-gates |

The three ACPI IOMMU parsers cannot run without overflowing the stack outright; they are reached at boot on any machine whose firmware publishes a DMAR or IVRS table, which QEMU's default aarch64 machine does not, so nothing has exercised them. The same three functions are 8040/6424/4488 on x86_64 -- so this is not one bad function, it is one body compiled far more expensively on aarch64. **None of these five was visible before this lane**; the arm baseline file is empty, so they are not tolerated waivers, they were unmeasurable. | B2248. `make frame-gate-arm` after the decoder fix. x86_64 comparison from the same tree's `frame-gate-x86`. | unowned |

### B1719-procfs-per-superblock-root

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | **Nothing in `procfs` can be tested through an actual mount.** `fs_impl`, `static_files` and `live` are all `#[cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]`, so `ProcfsFs::new` / `build_root` / the lookup and readdir enforcement have no hosted reach — a test written against them is a phantom, which is why this lane's mount test was deleted rather than shipped. ### B1720-fsmount-real-vfsmount

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **A `make feature-gate` can report GREEN for the wrong tree.** `cd <worktree> && (gate) & (smoke) & wait` parses as `{ cd && gate } &` then `{ smoke } &` — `&` binds looser than `&&` — so the second job runs in the shell's original directory, normally the MAIN tree. Observed here: the gate type-checked `main` and passed while this branch's kernel build was broken by a missing re-export (`E0425`), which only `make x86` caught. Not a defect in the gate itself (a correctly-targeted run catches it: exit=2 with the error) but a foot-gun that the newly-mandated parallel-checks rule makes MORE likely. `CLAUDE.md` now carries the shape and says to confirm the gate log names your worktree. A `make` guard that refuses to run when `$(CURDIR)` is not the git worktree root of the checked source would remove it entirely. | This lane, twice: gate-exit=0 with `Checking vfs (/home/nd/oxide/kernel/...)` in the log while the worktree was `/home/nd/oxide/kernel-B1720`; after the fix, gate-exit=0 with the worktree's own paths. | unowned |

### B1730-irq-nesting-unbounded

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | MISSING | med | **No interrupt-nesting depth bound and no runtime stack high-water mark.** Nesting is now structurally bounded at two levels, but nothing measures or enforces it, so a future change that reopens a window fails the same silent way. The reference reports a stack's deepest use (`CONFIG_DEBUG_STACK_USAGE`: zero-fill at allocation — which `kstack::alloc` already does — then scan upward from the low end to the first non-zero word, reported at task exit by `check_stack_usage`). Adding that plus an explicit nesting counter would make a regression measurable instead of fatal. Design worked out this lane: scan in `Drop for Task`, one global low-water atomic, and an on-demand sysrq key for the interrupt stack, which is never freed and so has no exit point. | Reference: `kernel/exit.c` `stack_not_used`/`check_stack_usage`; `kernel/fork.c` `GFP_VMAP_STACK` zero-fill. Our zero-fill is already at `kstack.rs` `alloc`. | unowned |

### B1731-failed-units-boot-health

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **`systemctl` intermittently cannot reach the service manager: `Failed to list units: Transport endpoint is not connected`.** Seen once ~75 s into a live-gnome boot, from a shell that had worked moments earlier; a later run at ~40 s queried cleanly. ENOTCONN on the manager's AF_UNIX socket means the peer went away or the connection was reset, which on a healthy system does not happen. Recorded because it silently turns a verification query into an empty result — an empty `systemctl --failed` was briefly mistaken for "nothing fails". Any probe that reads unit state must check for this line before believing the answer. | `verify2.log`: `Failed to list units: Transport endpoint is not connected`, with the same command succeeding in `verify3.log`. | unowned |
| OPEN | INFRA | med | **Boot probes were used to answer questions the source answers.** Roughly eight boots went into this lane, several of them to establish facts a grep would have given in seconds, and two produced no usable answer at all because the typed probe was mangled by interleaved kernel output or swallowed by `systemctl`'s pager. The repo's own rule says a harness beats a boot and that more than two boots chasing one bug means the loop is the bug. Practical notes for the next lane: pass `SYSTEMD_PAGER=cat` and `--no-pager` or the probe hangs in `less`; the alive-marker must not appear in the typed command (use the quote-split form); and `rc=$?` after a pipeline reports the LAST command's status, not the one being tested — that mistake produced a false `rc=0` for three mounts here. | This lane. | unowned |

### D513-real-hardware-findings

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |

| IN-PROGRESS B2111-xhci-linux-audit | MISSING | blocker | **Native PCI xHCI now exists and is registered before PCI driver probing:** class `0x0c0330`, IOMMU-mapped DMA pages, controller reset/run, MSI/MSI-X binding, root-port address/configure, USB HID boot keyboard/mouse reports, platform-keyed evdev publication, and root-port connect/disconnect handling through the USB softirq. The old claim that no xHCI or bus-agnostic input existed is false. USB mass-storage descriptor discovery, Bulk-Only/SCSI CBW/CSW contracts, generic bulk endpoint-context construction, retained requester-keyed bulk DMA owners, non-overwriting transfer-completion handoff, native configuration sequencing, native CBW/data/CSW transfer submission, initial INQUIRY/READ CAPACITY transaction sequencing, canonical `sd*` block-device publication, and page-vector payload transfers now exist. One transaction can map up to 255 IOMMU DMA pages and submits chained Normal TRBs with a completion only on the terminal buffer. Root addressing now flows through an xHCI topology owner that preserves a physical root port plus route string. Hub-class status endpoints are parsed, configured, submitted, completion-validated, DMA-invalidated, and rearmed through that same transport owner; the hub descriptor is fetched in two stages, then its protocol/TT facts update the xHCI hub slot context. Hub initialization powers every downstream port, waits its one descriptor-provided power-good interval, reads changed/initial port status in a process worker, acknowledges change features, resets connected ports, and publishes descendant slots with the parent route string. The port reset-control transaction drops controller/device locks before completion waits and recovery delay. Descendant detach and recursive topology are still absent. The adapter owns the SCSI disk reservation until detach, rejects reuse when the canonical registry cannot detach an open disk, and serializes every command through the controller-then-device transaction gate. The shared block registry owns the reusable SCSI `sd*` identity allocator, so SATA and USB storage do not publish through competing counters; a shared SCSI midlayer, full hub topology, HID report-descriptor support beyond boot protocol, and physical xHCI exercise remain. The Linux module facade remains a shim and is not the native-driver path. | `drv-xhci::{probe,probe_hub,storage_block,irq,completion,usb,storage,context,hid,device}`; `block::{registry,BlockDevice}`; `pci-boot::register_pci_model_drivers`; `input::{InputDeviceKey::platform,install,publish_evdev}`; `irq::take_port_changes` → `probe::service_port_changes`. | B2111-xhci-linux-audit; B2112-usb-mass-storage; B211301-xhci-bulk-endpoints; B2113-xhci-storage-dma; B2114-xhci-completion-queue; B2115-xhci-storage-probe; B2116-xhci-bot-execution; B2118-block-sd-name-allocator; B2119-xhci-controller-ownership; B2120-xhci-controller-handle; B2121-xhci-storage-transaction; B2122-xhci-storage-block-device; B2124-xhci-storage-scatter-gather; B2125-xhci-hub-topology; B2126-xhci-hub-endpoint; B2127-xhci-hub-port-control; B2128-xhci-hub-port-status; B2129-xhci-hub-child-enumeration |

A published HPET with no trustworthy scope is refused. Remaining closure is a real HPET clockevent owner using this message path instead of the current LAPIC-only timer path. | `firmware::{hpet_pa,hpet_id}`; `iommu::{intel_vtd_hpet_source,allocate_vtd_hpet,VtdIrte::hpet}`; `smoke/src/device_map/x86.rs` | B210101-vtd-hpet-interrupt-remapping |

Remaining scalar target consumers are tracked in the B2098 row and must move through the same mask-shaped owner before x2APIC is enabled. | `sched/src/membarrier/work.rs`; `cpu/src/mask.rs`; `mm-vmm/src/address_space.rs::cpumask_full` | B2101-x2apic-cpu-targeting |

B2097 adds VT-d's IRTE/IRTA/IRE path and routes PCI MSI/MSI-X through it when the owning unit supports it. B2098 now preserves DMAR's x2APIC opt-out policy and rejects EIM unless every published DRHD advertises queued invalidation, interrupt remapping, and EIM — the same all-IOMMU decision Linux makes. B2102 converts generic call-function/TLB, mm residency, LDT, direct-map, swap, pageout, TLB-gather, and kexec request targets to canonical CPU-mask words. B2107 gives architecture-owned stop, fault, TSS, and generic per-CPU storage one 256-slot transport bound without raising scheduler admission. B2108 converts task, scheduler, syscall, cgroup, io_uring, procfs, and deadline affinity state to canonical CPU-mask words. B2110 makes generic RCU consume the complete transport-width online set, so CPUs above bit 63 cannot be omitted from a grace period. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **A syscall call site that dereferences a USER address raw after a RANGE-only check has no exception-table recovery, so a user that unmaps the page under the syscall faults the kernel instead of getting `EFAULT`.** Local denial of service, reproducible the same way for every hit: map a page, `MADV_DONTNEED` it, race the syscall. The fix is mechanical for almost all of them — copy through `uaccess::{copy_from_user, copy_to_user}`, whose hand-written asm is the only thing that gets an `__ex_table` fixup. **D552 replaced the old candidate list with a swept, classified inventory** (`read_volatile`/`write_volatile`/`read_unaligned`/`copy_nonoverlapping` across `crates/kernel/**`, each hit classified by pointer ORIGIN): **~184 confirmed user-origin hits, ~175 of them mechanically convertible**, and **~74 apparent hits that are already CORRECT and must not be "fixed"** — chiefly io_uring/aio ring access and mm HHDM copies, which dereference the kernel's own alias of a kernel-owned frame, plus foreign-AS ptrace access, kernel trap-frame reads, and `uaccess::cstr` itself. Lane boundaries, by crate: (1) `syscalls/016_ioctl/*` — **CLOSED by B2051**: 124 raw-dereference occurrences across 11 files converted to `crate::ioctl_user`, whose every accessor is `uaccess`-backed; the tree greps clean under `016_ioctl/` for `read_volatile`/`write_volatile`/`read_unaligned`/`write_unaligned`/`copy_nonoverlapping`; (2) `syscalls/101_ptrace/{info,mem,regset,sig}` ~25 — **CLOSED by B2052**, all 25 converted (info 12, regset 9 incl. the aarch64 `NT_ARM_HW_BREAK`/`WATCH` field helpers, sig 5, mem 3), decision half extracted to the ungated `101_ptrace/user.rs` (15 hosted tests). `101_ptrace/frame.rs`'s two hits stay excluded — re-confirmed: their pointer is derived from `Task::kernel_stack`/`svc_frame`, kernel memory, not a user address. What remains reachable from ptrace is `signal_common::{read,write}_user_siginfo`, which GETSIGINFO/SETSIGINFO/PEEKSIGINFO call — boundary (3)'s; (3) ~60 hits spread over the remaining `syscalls` slot files and helpers (`iov`, `signal_common`, `clock_ops`, `aio/*`, `siocgif`, `statmount_target`, `tcp_info`, `mount_common`, `netlink_fd`, `fcntl_lease`, `pselect_ppoll_edge`); **(4) `fs` — CLOSED by B2054, 21 hits converted** (`epoll/fileops` 8, `epoll/syscalls` 5, `epoll/scan` 2, `fileattr` 3, `signalfd/syscalls` 1, `userfaultfd/ioctl/{structs,api,register}` 4+1, `watch_queue/ioctl` 1, `xattr/user` 2, plus `userfaultfd/work/install::fill_frame`); **(5) `ipc` — CLOSED by B2054, 16 hits converted** (`live/futex/core` 2, `live/futex/robust` 1, `live/posix_mq/user` 6, `live/posix_mq/wait` 2, `sysv_shm/shmctl` 4, `sysv/user` 2); (6) `sched` + `security/seccomp/user` + `syscall::numa` — **CLOSED by B2055** (22 hits: `cred/caps.rs` 9, `prctl/name.rs` 2, `prctl_set_mm.rs` 2, `security/seccomp/user.rs` 7, plus 7 more found in the same crate during the re-sweep, `sched/src/xfer.rs` `sendfile`'s `loff_t *offset` — not in the D552 inventory, and its range check covered only the FIRST byte of the 8. The `syscall::numa` hit was DEAD CODE: `route_b` claims `NR_GET_MEMPOLICY` before `route_c` ever sees it, so the live implementation is `syscalls::239_get_mempolicy`, which already copies through `uaccess`; the duplicate — which ignored every flag and answered `MPOL_DEFAULT` unconditionally — is deleted rather than converted). **NOT mechanical — needs judgement, do not blind-swap (9):** `016_ioctl/common::ioctl_file_dedupe_range` (variable-length dest array, count from user); `016_ioctl/fiemap::handle_fiemap` extent-write closure (invoked per-extent from an fs callback, not a flat copy); `fs/userfaultfd/work/install::fill_frame` (one upfront check then a multi-page loop — needs per-chunk copies); `ipc/live/futex/robust::read_user_u64` (deliberately avoids uaccess on the task-exit path, page-table presence check instead); `mm-vmm/mremap::mremap_full` (own row below); `security/seccomp/user::*` (that crate has no `uaccess` dependency yet). **Phantom-test hazard:** most of `016_ioctl/*` (except `blk.rs`/`common.rs`/`fileattr.rs`), all of `101_ptrace/*` and most slot files carry a whole-file `#[cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]`, so a test written beside them compiles to nothing and reports ok. `sched/prctl_set_mm.rs` is the exception — its gate includes `test`/`hosted`. **Two prior claims corrected:** the row's `202_futex.rs` hit is STALE (its only match is a comment; the real futex hits are in `ipc/live/futex/{core,robust}.rs`), and B1997/B2001 did close `038_setitimer`, `131_sigaltstack`, `128_rt_sigtimedwait` and `202_futex`'s timespec. `035_nanosleep` is still unconverted and needs its harness untangled first: `vfs/tests/conformance_misc.rs` and `fs/tests/sys_nanosleep_shape.rs` both `#[path]`-include the slot file and neither crate depends on `uaccess`. | D552 sweep of `crates/kernel/**`, every hit classified by pointer origin. | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **`cmpxchg_user_u32` performs an atomic read-modify-write DIRECTLY on a user address with no exception-table fixup**, so the PI-futex and robust-futex paths still fault the kernel when the word goes away — the one remaining raw user dereference in `ipc` after B2054. It is reached from `futex/pi/{lock,unlock,requeue,exit}.rs` and `futex/robust.rs`, each of which guards it with a `user_addr_accessible(uaddr, true)` page-table probe. The probe does not close the window: the futex word belongs to a live multi-threaded process whose PEERS can unmap it between the probe and the RMW, and on the robust/PI exit paths the guard is all that stands between a hostile userspace and a kernel fault. The reference has a dedicated fixup-carrying primitive for exactly this (`futex_atomic_cmpxchg_inatomic`), which returns `-EFAULT` instead of faulting. The fix is an arch asm routine per HAL — `lock cmpxchg` with an `__ex_table` entry on x86_64, `ldaxr/stlxr` with one on aarch64 — surfaced through `uaccess` beside `raw_copy_{from,to}_user`, after which every `user_addr_accessible(_, true)` call in `ipc` can go. | `crates/kernel/ipc/src/live/futex/core.rs::cmpxchg_user_u32` builds an `&AtomicU32` straight from the user VA; `crates/arch/hal-{x86_64,aarch64}/src/uaccess.rs` contains only the two byte-copy routines, so nothing else in the tree has a fixup. B2054 converted every plain read/write on these paths and left this one because it needs new asm, not a swap. | unassigned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **`uaccess` has no single-instruction `get_user`/`put_user`, so every scalar user access in the kernel pays a byte-copy routine — and on aarch64 that routine copies ONE BYTE PER ITERATION.** `oxide_raw_copy_from_user`/`_to_user` are `rep movsb` on x86_64 (acceptable for a 4-byte fetch, ~an order of magnitude over a single `mov`) and a `ldrb`/`strb` loop with a `subs`/`b.ne` on aarch64, which is byte-rate for EVERY copy in the kernel, not only the scalar ones — a `read(2)` of a page moves 4096 bytes one at a time. The reference has both: `__get_user`/`__put_user` are a single fixup-carrying load or store, and `copy_from_user` is a tuned bulk routine. Two separate wins: a `get_user_u32`/`put_user_u32`/`get_user_u64` family for the hot scalar paths (`futex_get_value_locked` is on FUTEX_WAIT, the hottest lock path in the system), and a word-at-a-time aarch64 bulk copy. | `crates/arch/hal-aarch64/src/uaccess.rs` — the whole body is `1: ldrb w3,[x1],#1; 2: strb w3,[x0],#1; subs x2,x2,#1; b.ne 1b`. B2054 converted `ipc`'s futex fetches to `copy_from_user` on a 4-byte stack array rather than add arch asm outside its lane; no bounce buffer is allocated, but the call is still the byte loop on ARM. | unassigned |

| FIXED B2022 | MISSING | med | Rows 60/231: per-OPEN tty hangup revocation. Implemented as a hangup GENERATION rather than a mutable `f_op` plus an open-file registry: the tty bumps `hup_gen` under the port lock on every `__tty_hangup`, each open samples it into `vfs::File::f_revoke_gen`, and read/write/poll/ioctl compare. A description open across a hangup reads EOF, writes EIO, polls the full `hung_up_tty_poll` mask and ioctls EIO (ENOTTY for TIOCSPGRP) for the rest of its life, including after any number of later opens of the same line; a description opened after the hangup is unaffected. `clear_bit(TTY_HUPPED)` on every successful open is KEPT — it is what makes the LINE work for the next session, and it is only safe because the old descriptions are retired independently. Wired on `/dev/tty*`, `/dev/tty0`, `/dev/console` and `/dev/ttyS0`. The pty half (`devpts` slave `clear_hangup`) is the same class and is NOT covered — see the row below. | B2022. `cargo test -p tty` 197 -> 207. | OPEN | MISSING | med | `TIOCSTI` is not implemented at all — a byte cannot be pushed into a terminal's input queue by ioctl. B2031 built and tested that record body (`producers::tty_body` with `TTY_DESC_TIOCSTI`) but installed no call site, because inventing the audit hook for an ioctl this kernel does not have would be machinery with no caller. | OPEN | MISSING | med | No per-task login uid or audit session id. `/proc/<pid>/loginuid` is a static file reading `4294967295` and `/proc/<pid>/sessionid` a static `0`; neither is backed by a field on the task or its credentials, and `loginuid` is not writable, so `pam_loginuid` cannot establish a login identity. Every `AUDIT_TTY` record B2031 emits therefore reports `auid=4294967295 ses=0` — deliberately the same values the two procfs files report, so the record and the process file cannot disagree, but an auditor cannot attribute terminal input to a human until the real fields exist. Closing it means a `loginuid`/`sessionid` pair on the credentials, the write path with its one-shot and `AUDIT_FEATURE_LOGINUID_IMMUTABLE` rules, inheritance across fork, and then converting both procfs leaves and the audit producer at once. | `procfs/src/live/pid_dir.rs` `pc_loginuid`/`pc_sessionid` are constant inodes; `fs/src/tty_audit.rs` `AUDIT_UID_UNSET`/`AUDIT_SESSION_UNSET`. | audit lane |

B2042 lands `fs::perf::throttle` (per-event interrupt count + per-CPU tick generation, park/release, group-wide park with the marker emitted against the leader, a periodic release tick registered from `emit::init`), the two record layouts in `fs::perf::sideband::record`, and the emit-path wiring: the first record of one counter-site burst is exempt and every one after it is charged, a timer-driven expiry is charged on its single record, and a parked event drops the whole opportunity rather than just its records. | FIXED F857 | DEFECT | med | Context-switch notes were parked ONE PER CPU and a newer switch overwrote an undrained one, so any CPU that took two switches before its bottom half ran delivered only the last — losing a `PERF_RECORD_SWITCH` pair outright, and (once notes drove accounting) leaving the skipped switch's outgoing thread counting an interval it did not run. Two switches between drains is ordinary: a thread that blocks again before the softirq runs produces exactly that. F857 makes the notes a per-CPU ring, drained oldest-first, refusing the newest on overflow so the undrained chain is never broken in the middle. | F857. `sched::perf_sw::notes`, 4 hosted tests. | OPEN | DEFECT | med | Process-group and session ids are NOT stored untranslated — they are stamped in vpid space at spawn/fork and stay self-consistent inside one pid namespace. ### B1979-gnome-segfault-regression-triage

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | High | A process that loads the GIO module `libdconfsettings.so` intermittently dies with `SIGSEGV` on the FIRST read of the dconf database it has just mapped. The faulting instruction is `mov (%rax),%edx` at module offset `0x5245`, where `%rax` is the pointer `g_mapped_file_new()` → `g_mapped_file_get_bytes()` → `g_bytes_get_data()` just handed back and the size check `cmpq $0x17,0x10(%rbx)` has already passed — so userspace holds a mapping the kernel then refuses to fault in. When the victim is `gnome-shell` the whole desktop dies to a bare console; when it is a session service (`evolution-source-registry`) the desktop survives. **Not introduced by the 2026-08-08 wave** — it reproduces on `5506b785e`, the last-known-good commit, at the SAME module offset. | Paired live-gnome boots, same host, same qemu invocation, run concurrently: `910470808` 0 faults / 5 boots, `5506b785e` 1 fault / 5 boots (plus one earlier `910470808` boot under a wider device set, which faulted — so 1/6 vs 1/5, indistinguishable at this sample size). Both fault lines carry `ino 0x6e54000000003e14` (ext4 inode 15892 = `/usr/lib64/gio/modules/libdconfsettings.so`) and `off 0x5245`; only the victim process and the ASLR bases differ. Screendump of the `5506b785e` boot is a black console holding the `gnome-shell` segfault line; every non-faulting boot on BOTH commits reaches the GNOME overview. Logs: `main-1`, `maina-1..5`, `basea-1..5` under the B1979 scratch dir. | unowned |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | med | Nothing in the tree measures whether a live-gnome boot reaches the desktop, so a desktop-visible regression is only ever found by a human looking at a screen. This triage had to build a one-off harness (boot the profile's ISO with a QMP socket, `screendump` at a fixed deadline, count `segfault at` lines in serial) to get a comparable number out of two commits. | `make` has smoke targets for login and for individual subsystems, none for `PROFILE=live-gnome` reaching `graphical.target`; `tools/boot-report.py` reports `graphical.target: no` for a boot whose serial console is taken over by `debug-shell.service`, which is every live-gnome boot driven through `tools/boot-capture.sh`. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | High | The observed victim was `libdconfsettings.so` +0x5245, `mov (%rax),%edx`, on the pointer `g_mapped_file_new()` returned with its size check already passed — the kernel handed userspace a file mapping and then refused to fault it in. When the victim is `gnome-shell` the desktop dies to a bare console. The row above fixes one mechanism that produces exactly this; whether it is the ONLY one is not established, because a failed fill and an absent mapping are indistinguishable in the log until B1985 lands. Keep open until a live-gnome run has gone long enough without it to be convincing. | `gnome-shell[398]: segfault at 00007f257af04000 ip 00007f257a65b245 ... ino 0x6e54000000003e14 (ext4 inode 15892) off 0x5245`. Faulting instruction disassembled from the module. | unowned |
| OPEN | INFRA | med | Three harness traps, each of which produced a wrong answer here. (1) The one boot that faulted during A/B was also the only one of ten to log `[WATCHDOG] soft lockup: no reschedule for 10s` and `no-progress: 0 context switches for 40s` — host swap was 7/7 GB full under two concurrent 4 GB guests, so contention manufactures the fault and any paired-boot comparison is invalid. (2) `nvme-<arch>.img`/`ahci-<arch>.img` are created by `xtask grub` at boot, NOT by `xtask image`, so a hand-rolled qemu line exits in under a second while a sleep-based harness counts it as a clean boot. (3) `tools/boot-capture.sh` runs `pkill -9 -f qemu-system`, which kills every other lane's boot on this shared box. | Logs under the B1979 scratch dir; `free -g` during the run; `image_qemu/common.rs::ensure_storage_img`. | unowned |

### B1985-fault-fill-failure-reports-sigbus

That contract holds for a memcg charge failure (`mm-pmm/src/memcg.rs` `MemoryPressure::Max` reclaims, then `sched::oom::kill_memcg`), and does NOT hold for plain frame exhaustion: `alloc_one_frame` returning `None` kills nothing, so the instruction re-faults forever. Closing this means a global OOM selector on the allocator failure path, not a change to the fault classification. | `crates/kernel/mm-vmm/src/fault_signal.rs` `FaultFailure::Oom`; the only OOM selector in the tree is `sched::oom::kill_memcg`, reached solely from the cgroup pressure hook (grep: no other caller). Previously this reason terminated the task with SIGSEGV, which is not what the reference reports. | unowned |

### B1986-main-aarch64-stack-overrun

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | DEFECT | high | **The socket destructor runs inline on whoever drops the last reference, so a 4,544 B teardown cascade is charged to every path in the kernel that can touch a socket.** (D551: `stack-gate` is GREEN on both arches as of 44765839c, so this is no longer a failing gate — it is a deep path with ~4 KB of headroom left, and the gate will fail on the next thing that lands on top of it.) `Drop for InetSocket` calls `release_file`, and from there: `release_file` 352 -> `tcp_fastopen::drain_client` 624 -> `net_ns::state::materialize_state` 1,168 -> `NsNet` / `UnixListener` / `UnixPair` drops -> `Arc<Task>` -> FdTable -> `filp_close` -> `File` -> mount -> `MntNamespace` -> `generic_shutdown_super` -> writeback. The reference does not do this: `__sk_free` hands `__sk_destruct` to `call_rcu`, so the destructor runs from an RCU callback in process context. **RETRACTION (B1996): the `in_interrupt()` branch in `release_file` is NOT a split truth and must not be "unified" away.** It is the reference's own distinction between close-time protocol shutdown (`inet_release`: process context, synchronous, takes RTNL) and final destruction from an arbitrary context (`__sk_destruct`: deferred). Making the deferral unconditional was tried and is WRONG — it breaks the contract that a close releases its device references immediately, and seven hosted tests assert exactly that (`final_close_flushes_every_unique_device_reference`, `process_context_final_drop_still_releases_inline`, `socket_anycast_is_not_multicast_and_close_releases_device_ref`, and four more). Closing this row is therefore NOT about when the destructor runs. It is about making the destructor path SHALLOW: the deep frames under it (`tcp_fastopen::drain_client` 624, `materialize_state` 1,168) have to shrink, or the owned state has to be moved out of the socket into a queued bundle so the drop itself is a hand-off. Routing individual `Arc<InetSocket>` drop sites through the reaper was also tried and does not work: the destructor edge exists at many sites (`packet_fanout::rollover` keeps it even after `packet::deliver` is routed), the gate got WORSE (13,024 -> 13,040), and the same seven tests fail. | B1995, measured on aarch64 at `621c7cfe8`: `Arc<InetSocket>::drop_slow` subtree = 4,544 B, reached on the ceiling path from `packet::deliver`'s upgraded socket references. It is larger than every net transmit frame on that path combined. | unowned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | med | **`net_ns::state::materialize_state` reserves 1,168 B of stack to insert one map entry.** The body is a `BTreeMap` lookup and an `Arc::new(NsNet::new())`; the frame is that large because `NsNet` is built as a stack temporary and then copied into the `Arc` allocation. It is the single largest frame on the aarch64 ceiling path and it is reached from a destructor. Any large structure constructed by value and then moved into an allocation has the same shape, so the fix is a construction pattern, not one call site. | B1995. `stack-depth-gate --show-path` on the `Arc<InetSocket>::drop_slow` subtree: `materialize_state` +1,168, against a body whose own work is O(log N) on a map. | unowned |
| OPEN (PARKED — do not re-attack without a new idea) | DEFECT | high | **The aarch64 deepest static call path sits at the `stack-gate` ceiling, so a lane that touches any widely-used crate turns the gate red on codegen drift alone — five lanes have now pushed with `SKIP_STACK_GATE=1` for this one chain.** Measured at 12,992 B of a 13,000 B ceiling on a 16,384 B stack (8 B of margin), and over it at 13,072-13,120 B on branches that edit no net or io_uring file. **The reducible mass is the net/socket transmit chain, NOT the syscall entry or io_uring submission:** of the 12,992, entry costs 288 (aarch64 `pt_regs`, irreducible), `oxide_syscall_dispatch` 304, `sys_io_uring_enter` 128, `submit_sqes` 416 and the pwritev/writev shims 240 — 1,376 total — while ~7,000 B is the `socket::send` / `net::stack_ipv6` / `net::netdev` transmit chain and a further 4,608 B sits under `net::sock::packet_fanout::rollover`. Splitting the entry frames measurably makes the path DEEPER. The reference keeps a chain like this under its own ceiling by marking the deep-but-cold frames so they cannot be inlined into their caller (`noinline_for_stack`) and by moving large locals off the stack, which is the shape the fix takes here. **CORRECTION, measured across B1992-B1994: the switch tail was a real 3.4 KiB cost on every blocking path, but it is NOT on this gate's maximum path — the row's original attribution to the net transmit frames was right.** The tail ran the dying task's exit notification and the teardown its registry snapshot opens; that is now gone (B1992 hands the final reference to a process-context drainer, B1993 releases the kernel stack in the tail so reaping can no longer free a running task's stack, B1994 moves the notification onto the dying task's own exit path where the reference runs `exit_notify`). Blocking tail 3,536 -> 2,592 B, charged to every path in the kernel that can block. The ceiling path did not move by one byte — 13,024 B before and after — because it never included the tail. **The remaining work is the net frames named below and nothing else:** `packet::deliver` 864, `send_inet` 736, `TxDispatch::enqueue` 736, `sendto_v6_ctl` 608, `send_udp6_pmtu_to_bound_opts_owner` 592, `xmit_ipv6_with_extensions` 560, plus the `Arc<InetSocket>` teardown the AF_PACKET tap opens under `packet_fanout::rollover`. | Chain, clean aarch64 rebuild of `14b9a7828`: `oxide_svc_save_block` 12992 -> `oxide_syscall_dispatch` +304 -> `sys_io_uring_enter` +128 -> `submit_sqes` +416 -> `do_pwritev` +48 -> `sys_writev` +192 -> `socket::send::writev` +320 -> `send_retained` +400 -> `send_prepared` +416 -> `send_inet` +736 -> `sendto_inner` +448 -> `sol_udp::emit::{intercept,flush}` +192/+192 -> `sendto_v6_ctl` +608 -> `send_udp6_pmtu_to_bound_opts_owner` +592 -> `xmit_ipv6_payload_with_policy` +256 -> `xmit_ipv6_with_extensions` +560 -> `emit_ipv6_fragment` +160 -> `TxDispatch::enqueue` +736 -> `TxJob::transmit` +272 -> `deliver_packet_link_receive` +272 -> `packet::deliver` +848 -> `packet_fanout::rollover` +112 (subtree 4608). Same chain on x86_64 sums to 11,432 at `submit_sqes`, so this is aarch64-specific. Reproduced identically on `origin/main` in a detached worktree (`9c58dd70d`, `d605a3613`, `ff87ec195`) — inherited by every branch, caused by none of them. Folded from six rows filed separately by B1980, B1981, B1983, B1985, B1986 and B1988. | net/socket — PARKED after three approaches; see the retraction and both negative results before touching it |
| OPEN | COVERAGE | low | Negative result, recorded so the next lane does not re-run it: the io_uring submission frames are at their floor and **splitting them makes the gate worse**, because the ~30 opcode arms already coalesce into one well-coloured frame. Marking `io_uring::dispatch::router::run` `#[inline(never)]` moved the deepest path 12992 → **13024** (FAIL); marking `dispatch_op` `#[inline(never)]` moved it to **13136** (FAIL, `submit_sqes` 416 → 320 + a new 240 B `dispatch_op` frame). Splitting the `oxide_syscall_dispatch` exit tail into an `#[inline(never)]` `syscall_exit_work` — the mirror of the `syscall_entry_work` split a prior lane made — was exactly neutral (12992, dispatch frame still 304), so the exit work was already overlapping the routes rather than summing with them. ### B1987-oom-fault-leg-livelocks

| Status | Class | Sev | Issue | Evidence | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPEN | MISSING | med | The allocation slowpath bounds the number of out-of-memory selector invocations one allocation may make (`mm-pmm::oom_entry::MAX_OOM_ATTEMPTS`, 16) and then reports `NoMem`. The reference has no such bound — it retries for as long as the killer reports progress — which is safe there only because a reaper frees the victim's memory asynchronously, so "progress" becomes free pages within a bounded time. Without a reaper an unbounded loop here would spin against a victim that will not die, so the bound is a stand-in for the reaper and must be removed once one exists. | `mm-pmm/src/oom_entry.rs::after_oom`; `oom_entry::tests::kill_invocations_are_bounded_per_allocation` pins the current behaviour so its removal is visible | unassigned |
| OPEN | MISSING | HIGH | No OOM reaper and no give-up on a victim that will not die. The "a victim is already exiting, kill nothing more" rule has no escape hatch: a victim wedged in an uninterruptible sleep leaves the scan reporting in-progress forever, so a later exhaustion can never select anyone and the fault leg retakes indefinitely. The reference reaps the victim's mm asynchronously and, when that fails, marks the mm skippable so selection resumes. | `oom::select::select_victim` returns `InProgress` for as long as `Task::oom_victim` is set and the task is alive; `oom::tests::the_scope_resumes_selecting_once_the_victim_has_gone` shows selection only resumes when the victim actually reaches zombie | unassigned |
| OPEN | MISSING | MED | No coredump-in-progress state on the thread group, so the "already dying" shortcut cannot exclude a process that is dumping core. The reference refuses to treat a coredumping process as one that will promptly free memory, because the dump can sleep for a long time; here such a process short-circuits the whole selection to `SelfWillFree` and nothing is killed. | `sched::oom::kill::will_free_mem` checks group-exit latch + pending SIGKILL only; no `core_state` equivalent exists anywhere in `crates/kernel/sched/src` | unassigned |
| OPEN | DEFECT | LOW | An OOM kill is not reported on a production console. The reference prints the victim unconditionally; `04§4.0` is frozen on every `klog` call site being `cfg`-elidable, so the report sits behind `debug-sched`. The kill is still counted (`/proc/vmstat` `oom_kill`, victim cgroup `memory.events` `oom_kill`), so it is observable — but a service that vanished under pressure leaves no console record. | `sched/src/oom/kill.rs::report` under `#[cfg(all(target_os = "oxide-kernel", feature = "debug-sched"))]`; the ungated form regresses the `crates/kernel/sched [code/klog-ungated]` ratchet key 163 → 168 | unassigned |

| OPEN | COVERAGE | MED | The expedited-round IPI protocol has no check that can fail. The round KIND is stored Release BEFORE `PENDING`; reversing the two stores lets a target run a plain barrier for a SYNC_CORE round and silently drop the core-serialization guarantee, and nothing hosted or booted would notice. The ACK wait and the single-in-flight `OWNER` handoff are equally unpinned. LANE SCOPE: model the sender/target pair under loom (the repo already uses loom elsewhere) — one sender publishing KIND+PENDING against one or more targets running `service()`, asserting that any target which clears its bit observed the round's own KIND. Positive control: reverse the two stores and confirm the model goes RED. Extend to two concurrent senders for the `OWNER` handoff. | `crates/kernel/sched/src/membarrier/work.rs` — `KIND.store(..., Release)` then `PENDING.store(targets, Release)`; `service()` reads KIND only after an Acquire load of `PENDING` saw its own bit. The kind dispatch is `Kind::from_u32(KIND.load(Relaxed))`. | unowned |

| FIXED B2006 | DEFECT | HIGH | Writing ONE block into a `fallocate(2)` preallocation zeroed and converted the ENTIRE unwritten extent, so a journal-writer's first log line cost one device write per preallocated block and consumed the whole preallocation. | OPEN | DEFECT | MED | ext4 drops the `datasync` argument: `Ext4RegFileOps::fsync` and `Ext4StatFileOps::fsync` both take `_datasync` and discard it, so `fdatasync(2)` always pays a full mount-wide metadata commit plus an unconditional device barrier. The reference waits only on the inode's `i_datasync_tid` and skips the commit entirely when that transaction already committed, and issues the barrier only when its own commit is the one owing it. Three linked divergences: datasync ignored; the commit drains the whole mount's shadow map rather than this inode's transaction; the barrier is unconditional (a 4th flush on top of the 3 the WAL already issues) and ignores `nobarrier`. NOTE: gating the barrier on "did the commit write anything" is NOT a valid fix — it is only accidentally safe because `write_at_inner` unconditionally restages the inode block, so any future skip of that restage, or any data path not going through `write_at`, silently loses durability. The Linux-shaped fix needs a per-mount commit generation plus per-inode `i_sync_tid`/`i_datasync_tid` and a barrier watermark. | `crates/kernel/ext4/src/rootfs/inode/regular.rs:241`, `special.rs:284`, `ext4_sync_file` at `regular.rs:28-35`, `mount/batch.rs::commit_batch`. Reference: `commit_tid = datasync ? i_datasync_tid : i_sync_tid`. | unowned |

Every exit terminates, so that loop is not the spin. What IS established: the kernel is not tick-preemptive in kernel mode (the timer IRQ only sets `need_resched`), and `charge_exec_runtime` runs only from `update_curr()` inside `schedule()` — which is why the victim shows 42ms cputime after a 10s stall, and why 'low cputime' must NOT be read as 'starved'. | OPEN | DEFECT | low | An io_uring region is one CONTIGUOUS buddy run; the reference allocates it as an unordered page vector reached through a kernel virtual mapping. Order rounding therefore wastes up to just under half a region (a 3-page rings region occupies an order-2, 4-page run), and a deep ring needs an order-9 (2 MiB) run per region, which can fail with ENOMEM under fragmentation where the reference would succeed. Contiguity is forced by `VmaBacking::KernelFrame`, which names a region by its first PA; a page-vector backing in `mm-vmm` would remove both costs. The justification recorded in `syscalls/src/unconfigured.rs` — parity with a reference built WITHOUT those configs — is a divergence recorded as compliance, and it also contradicted `docs/15`, which fixes the OBSOLETE set at 17 numbers and says nothing else qualifies. Rows reclassified `MISSING` (new legend status: the refusal is OURS, not the reference's) and the ENOSYS remains only until each surface is built. NOTE the engineering reason in that file is SOUND and is why the refusal stands today — for `iopl`/`ioperm` there is no TSS I/O permission bitmap, so a success return would be a security lie — but it describes missing work, not Linux parity. `iopl`/`ioperm` claimed by lane B-iopl-ioperm-io-permission-bitmap (building the enforcement). `modify_ldt` needs a per-mm LDT + GDT descriptor + `lldt` on address-space switch; `kexec_load`/`kexec_file_load` need image staging plus `reboot(LINUX_REBOOT_CMD_KEXEC)`, which currently answers EINVAL. | Reference: `arch/x86/kernel/ldt.c`, `arch/x86/kernel/ioport.c`, `kernel/kexec.c`, `kernel/kexec_file.c` all carry real SYSCALL_DEFINEs.
